# Batch Summaries

One entry per completed batch, newest last. This is the durable home the quality gate needs: `logs/` is gitignored, so a batch summary written only into a phase log leaves no record for the next writer.

Rules for an entry:

- Write it after the chapters are finished, not before, and do not pad it to a length. Six to ten lines is usually right.
- Say what the batch **changed**, not what it was about: the new situation at the end, the cost paid, the promise made or broken, and the one question the next batch inherits.
- Record any canon the batch invented that later batches must obey, and put the durable part in `state/continuity.md` rather than leaving it only here.
- The one-line `Last batch summary` field in `state/current.md` names the most recent entry in this file.

## How to read this file, and the rolling window it now sits in

**This file is a rolling window. It holds the whole of the volume currently being written, one entry per completed batch, newest last, and nothing older. Everything older is in `state/archive/batch-summaries-volumes-01-12.md`, verbatim, with every entry exactly as it was here.** The window moved for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close and for Volumes 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, **and after that cut this file holds the guide and nothing else.** As of the review of Batch 0004 this file was **1.03 MB**; it was about 100 KB through Volume 07 and **is now about 3 KB, which is the size a rolling summary is supposed to be.** **Nothing is deleted, only moved, and nothing is renumbered, and a volume is never archived while a close is reading it, so the window moved for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close and not before.**

**The live window is empty and opens on Volume 09. The entries for Volumes 01 to 08, including all eight volume closes and every review-repair entry, are in `state/archive/batch-summaries-volumes-01-12.md` under the same headings and the same numbering.**

**A batch summary is the durable record of a batch, and `logs/` is gitignored, so a summary written only into a phase log leaves no record at all for the next writer.** That is why this file exists and why it is not to be trimmed for length. What may be trimmed is the *age* of an entry, and only by moving it to the archive, never by deleting it.


## The window, at the Volume 13 close

**The window moved forward for Volumes 01 to 12 at the Volume 13 close, and it is the first move in this project that TRIMS the live file as well as copying it.** Every one of the five earlier moves, at the Volume 07, 08, 09, 11 and 12 closes, took an exact copy into `state/archive/` and then left the live file whole, and the live file grew back from the copy point every time. **The consequence of that convention, measured, is this: at the moment this cut was taken the five ledgers stood at 3.06 MB together, `state/continuity.md` at 984 KB holding every volume from Volume 09 forward, `state/open-threads.md` at 764 KB, `state/chapter-summaries.md` at 560 KB, `state/batch-summaries.md` at 468 KB, and `state/current.md` at 246 KB holding ten overlapping "What is true at the close of Chapter N" blocks for Chapter 560 through Chapter 650. That is roughly 780,000 tokens of state for a project whose live instruction is about 25,000 words, and Volume 14 cannot load it. The review of Volume 13 Batch 0005 recorded the arithmetic and called it a blocker, and it is one, and the fix is this cut.**

**Nothing is lost and nothing is renumbered.** `state/archive/batch-summaries-volumes-01-12.md` is a byte-for-byte copy of this file as it stood immediately before this cut, so the entire pre-cut ledger, guides and all, is recoverable by concatenating the archive back onto the live file at the cut point, and item 412 and thread 412 and chapter 412 and batch 412 all mean exactly what they meant. **The cut moved whole sections only, at section boundaries, and it did not touch a single item number, thread number, chapter number or batch number.** What left the live file is: **`## The window, at the Volume 08 close` and every entry for Volumes 09, 10, 11 and 12, ending with *Volume 12, closed*.**

**What stayed live, and rule 4 of `state/archive/README.md` is why.** `## Hard canon`, `## Ending constraints` and `## Knowledge control` are global and are in this file now whatever their byte offset, and so are the two canon-clarification sections and `## Initial story threads`, `## Answers to the questions the outline phase had to settle`, `## Final-volume questions` and `## Planning status` in the thread ledger. They are in the archive as well, because the archive is a copy, and a global section in both places is harmless and a global section in neither is not.

**The rule this cut changes, stated so that nobody inherits it by accident.** The old convention was *copy, and leave the live file whole*, and its stated justification was that a copy is safer than a deletion. **That is true and it is also what made the files unusable, and both halves cannot stand.** The new convention is *copy first, verify the copy, then trim the live file*, and the verification is one command: the archive must be byte-identical to the pre-cut live file, which is checked here against git rather than by reading. **The window still does not move in the middle of a volume.** It moved for Volumes 01 to 07 at the Volume 07 close, 01 to 08 at the Volume 08 close, 01 to 09 at the Volume 09 close, 01 to 10 at the Volume 11 close, 01 to 11 at the Volume 12 close, and 01 to 12 at this one, and it will not move again until Volume 14 is closed.

**What a Volume 14 writer reads, in this order, and does not read the rest of:** this guide, `state/current.md`, `outline/volumes/volume-13-close.md` in whole, and the threads this close hands on. Everything older is reachable by grep and is not to be read. **The highest item in this file is the newest, and `state/current.md` carries the figure.**

## Volume 13, Batch 0001 — Chapters 601 to 610, "The Cheapest Thing on the List", days 1831 to 1872, 20,333 words in ten chapters

**The first block of a new volume, and the volume's plan was written in the same pass. `outline/volume-13.md` is *The Law of Unopened Doors*, Chapters 601 to 650, day 1831 to day 2040, five blocks of forty-two days, and the year turns inside it at day 1956.** The block is **20,333 words in ten chapters** on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file counted on its own and the ten added, per chapter **1,981 / 1,742 / 1,753 / 2,154 / 2,144 / 2,106 / 2,268 / 1,922 / 2,228 / 2,035**, and scene bodies **1,603 / 1,362 / 1,365 / 1,739 / 1,744 / 1,703 / 1,871 / 1,515 / 1,816 / 1,609**. **The manuscript is 1,702,034 in six hundred and ten chapters and Volumes 01 to 12 are 1,681,701 in six hundred and do not move.**

**What happens in it, in one paragraph.** A sheet arrives in a band of about a hundred and four on a Friday the room was shut, and it carries a name at the top and no sender, and it directs that a keeper be stood down for nine days and a road be shut for the same nine days. **A man with a badge refuses to call it false, and says the true thing and then the worse true thing.** The room's own three unmerged series put two contradictory warnings about one four miles of channel into two different books, and the copyist says out loud that the rule is the problem and that she will not break it. Seven roads are named once in a room with no list on it, and there is no eighth, and he says so. **A culvert at the town's gate has a course laid dry across a bed that had already gone, by men from the district, on an instruction with a name at the top of it.** A man of about forty-seven brings a page of seven lines, six of them marked, and says the block's truest sentence about a gap. **The sheet is obeyed. Nine days pass. Nothing rots, nobody is late, nobody is hurt, and a woman of about fifty comes four days of carting to say that the whole of what it cost is that for nine days nobody knew whether she had been stood down or had gone home for a bad week.** And a woman of about forty who runs a lime kiln asks a man of thirty-two with a limp **do you want it**, twice, and he does not answer.

**The three findings, and they are the whole of the block.** A document with a name at the top and no sender is obeyed, and the reason it is obeyed is true and nobody in nine counties has a better one. The room's virtue is a wall behind a road and the wall hid a contradiction for three weeks. And a road with no number is not a light thing; it is a thing with no weight in it, and a gap is not carried by anybody.

**The instruments, every basis named.** Ten money columns at **214, 232, 198, 245, 249, 182, 217, 260, 225 and 213 pence**, 8 to 12 items a column, every one footing on item sum, restated price list, printed total and mark conversion at forty-eight, ten distinct, no price above forty-one, no price of thirty-six, **no barred numeral in any total, over-figure or price, and 0 collisions against a 139-element prior set rebuilt as the union of two extractors over the six hundred chapters before this block, with the set size printed beside the claim because a collision report without its set is not a measurement.** Calendar re-derived from day 930 with the two halves derived separately, days **1831, 1837, 1841, 1846, 1851, 1854, 1858, 1861, 1870 and 1872**, **0 / 0** on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market claim, five market days and two Sundays on which the room is shut. `about` **519 at 25.53 per 1,000**, `the whole of` 22, `about four people` 7, filler words 23. Punctuation **16 / 0 / 0 / 0**. Curly quotes **228 and 228**, straight double quotes 0, curly apostrophes 0, straight apostrophes 42. Barred families, the retired reporting frame and `continuation` at **0**. Duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, against the six hundred before and against themselves: **0 and 0**. Thank-family **28, every one a negation or about four houses owing a lime bill.** `Aren Kest` 45, in every chapter. Name budget **2 of 5**, `Nell Craddock` and `Garvin Slade`, three slots unspent.

**The two repairs this pass made after measuring, and both are of a kind the house allows.** Four curly apostrophes in two chapters brought back to the house's straight form, and one barred token: `chapter-0604.md` had *failed* twice in one speech about a culvert bed and now reads *gone*, which is the truer word for masonry. **The one defect found and deliberately not repaired is `chapter-0600.md`, whose cost coda prints eight figures of time over a list of nine; Volume 12 is closed and `state/open-threads.md` 426 forbids a later batch repairing it, so it stands and is recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1451 for whoever owns the Volume 13 close.**

**Nobody thanked anybody in any of the ten chapters and nobody was thanked, and the block ends on a question of two words asked out loud on a slope by a woman with a barrow in her hands and not answered.**

## Volume 13, Batch 0002 — Chapters 611 to 620, "Three Blanks and Three Marks", days 1896 to 1914, 24,843 words in ten chapters, after a verification and repair pass that added the block's fifth beat to Chapter 0619 and a review repair that then repaired three defects that pass had certified clean across Chapter 0619, Chapter 0620 and Chapter 0610

**The escalation. The block is 24,843 words in ten chapters** on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file counted on its own and the ten added, per chapter **2,757 / 2,403 / 2,324 / 2,240 / 2,189 / 2,381 / 2,206 / 2,120 / 3,423 / 2,800**, and scene bodies **2,338 / 1,993 / 1,912 / 1,818 / 1,780 / 1,954 / 1,798 / 1,698 / 2,939 / 2,346**. **The basis for a body is the one already in `workspace/volume-13/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §80 — *the scene body located structurally as everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda* — which includes the title line and the dateline and is not in fact a scene body. **The repair briefly held that the series had been carried without a basis, tried three and failed, and was wrong; the finding is withdrawn and recorded at `state/continuity.md` 1475.** The real defect was stale figures. The manuscript is 1,726,877 in six hundred and twenty chapters, and Volumes 01 to 12 are 1,681,701 in six hundred and do not move, and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 reproduces the new total.** Its ten datelines are **days 1896, 1900, 1902, 1904, 1907, 1909, 1910, 1911, 1913 and 1914**, five market days, two Sundays on which the room is shut, and 1896 is the first of a month, which is the day the man of about forty-seven said he would come back on.

**What happens in it, in one paragraph.** The man of about forty-seven comes back on the first of a month as he said he would, and the page he left folded in four is not the page he brings: it is a bigger sheet, printed twice, with **six ruled spaces** against six lines and a seventh line bare, and it is going round nine counties, and about nine people at a counter under a weigh-house on a Sunday cannot put a figure in the seventh space and one woman tells him why in four days of carting. **A page comes back from a county with one mark in it, and the copyist says out loud that the keeper of the room did not come down to the lane, and nobody in that room agrees with her, and then she says the thing the volume is about: nothing in that room depends on her being in it.** A man of about thirty-one from the road office reads the room's practice correctly and says the rule is a wall in front of the only door there is, and the copyist is not defended, does not want to be, and says out loud that there is no second practice in the room to replace the one she has just called a blind spot. **A man of about fifty who lays stone comes after the frost and finds that the course laid dry across the bed of the culvert is gone and that under it there is a scour a foot and a half deep, and he lays three courses and refuses to lay a fourth, and he will not have it written down, and a boy of about seventeen is paid a penny out of nine hands and nobody says it was well done.** A man of about thirty-six walks four days for a figure for a road nobody has been asked to number and is told correctly that the figure is not the room's to give, that it has to be said out loud by a person who walks it and can be moved off it, and that the room is not going to ask, **and the answer is right and is nothing at all to a man who is four days on foot.** **And in a lane, a man of about thirty-four with a sheet shows Aren Kest a copy of the forged page and the name at the top of it is his own, in a hand that is not his, and he says nothing, and the man with the sheet writes on the back of the copy that he was shown it and said nothing, unasked.** On a Sunday a man of about forty-seven is right for the second time and the rightest fix anybody will be offered this winter is a one-day delay between a room and a gate, **and the delay costs a man of about thirty-four with a sheet the whole of his office, because his office is the day, and nobody thanked the man of about forty-seven for it and nobody blamed the man of about thirty-four either, and there is no head in nine counties on which to rule a delay.** A woman of about fifty walks about nine hundred paces along a made road to ask for a ditch, which is not a hearing and is not a figure and is not a form, and a man of about fifty cuts it because the water is coming over his road and nobody asked him to. **And at the gate at the low end of her own road, she asks what her own line is for, learns that her line is the sixth and that the sixth is assumed and that the page says nothing whatever about a road it is asking six people to keep, reads nineteen columns of her own count out loud at a gate in front of about nine people, puts a mark in the seventh space, and says that one time is one time and nobody may cite it.** The register takes its eighth entry, a question, and no finding goes under it, **and the copyist says out loud in a room with nine people in it that she is the hand that put the nine into the last column of a slate in an apron about six weeks ago and has not told anybody, and nobody thanked her and nobody told her she was wrong.**

**The four findings, and they are the whole of the block.** **A blank is not a no, and a road everybody has refused has been shut by a printing press.** **A figure about a road is the only thing anybody can be argued with, and the room cannot supply one, refuses correctly, and the refusal helps nobody, and the chapter says so on the page.** **A rule with no head on it is worth exactly nothing however right it is, and the one-day delay is the cheapest correct fix in nine counties and it has nowhere to go and it costs a man his work.** **And a person who has offered one thing once, for a reason, about her own road, has not started anything anybody else may continue.** And underneath all four: **the man who can see the load-bearing problem said the argument out loud in a lane and it did not make a thing true, because a thing said in a lane by him is a direction, and a direction is a page with a name at the top of it, and he has watched one shut a road for nine days.**

**The instruments, every basis named. THE `about` FIGURE IN THIS PARAGRAPH IS PRE-REPAIR AND IS SUPERSEDED BY THE REVIEW REPAIR SECTION AT THE END OF THIS ENTRY, which restates the block at 24,843 and `about` at 671 and 27.01; the money columns did not move and were not re-measured.** Ten money columns at **202, 215, 229, 234, 243, 251, 262, 271, 283 and 294 pence**, 9 to 11 items a column, every one footing on item sum, restated price list, printed total and mark conversion at forty-eight, ten distinct, no duplicated price inside a column, no price above forty-one, no price of thirty-six, **no barred numeral in any total, over-figure or price, and 0 collisions against a 167-element prior set rebuilt in this pass as the union of three independent extractors, one keyed on the item list's footing, one on the restated enumeration and one on the mark conversion, over the six hundred and ten chapters before this block, with the set size and the extractor count printed beside the claim because a collision report without its set is not a measurement and a free-totals list without its extractor is worse.** Calendar re-derived from day 930 with the two halves derived separately, **0 / 0 / 0** on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market claim, **and the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was found to be wrong in five rows and internally inconsistent after day 1872, and was not used and was not edited.** `about` **656 at 27.54 per 1,000**, `the whole of` 25, `about four people` 10, filler words 38, of which `wrote` is 24. Punctuation **1 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0**. Curly quotes **278 and 278**, straight double quotes 0, curly apostrophes 0, straight apostrophes 44. Barred families, the four barred numerals, the retired reporting frame and `continuation` at **0**. Duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, against the six hundred and ten before and against themselves: **0 and 0**; **longest run shared with the six hundred and ten before is forty words, three instances, all in 0611 and 0616, and all three are the cost coda's own frame.** Thank-family **31, every one a negation or a statement that a person had not said a thing in order to be thanked.** `Aren Kest` 43, in every chapter, at 1 in the two chapters in which he is four days down the made road. Name budget **2 of 5, three slots unspent, and this block spent none**, on the grounds that a block which can be carried by two established names and about nine people described by age and trade should hold a name back while there is still a volume left to spend one in.

**The four prose repairs this pass made after measuring.** A capitalised number word at the head of one cost coda's time list, which the capitalised two-word sweep cannot see and which the Volume 12 close recorded eleven of; a doubled emphasis marker spliced into a paragraph in 0620; four cost codas whose claimed figure did not foot against its own list, in all four of which the list was right and the numeral moved and never the other way round; and **one real lift, forty consecutive words in 0614 taken verbatim from 0604's closing paragraph, the same man at the same culvert at the same hour, rewritten to say the same four facts in its own order.** No chapter was restarted, no day or weekday moved, no money total moved, no name spent, no plot beat moved, no ending altered.

**The fifth beat, and the repair pass that put it on the page.** The block was found complete on disk by a later verification pass, with its summaries, its continuity items and its successor prompt all already written, and the pass verified every instrument against the files and found exactly one real defect, which was against `outline/volume-13.md` §8 Block 2 and not against the prose. That section requires that **the seven lines get three refusals and two signatures and one road that agrees to be shut and agrees to be shut in a way that is not a closure, because it asks for a count first**, and a whole-block sweep returned zero instances of it in any form: the page stood at three marks and three blanks and none of the three marks was conditional, because the sixth was the woman of about fifty, who explicitly refuses the reading in 0619 by saying a count shuts nothing and that nobody may cite hers. **The repair added fifteen paragraphs to `chapter-0619.md`, in the gate scene that already held the setup, immediately after the exchange in which the woman of about fifty says *nobody may cite it* and immediately before the paragraph that strikes the page's tally, so that the tally now accounts for a mark whose character the reader has just been given.** A woman of about forty-three who keeps a road that is not one of the six says her line is the fifth and that her road will stand aside, is told the cost, gives it in a working voice as the rest of it rather than nine days, and sets the condition: if her name goes in that space she wants the count taken first, by the people who walk it, on the ground, before her line is settled. **What her road keeps is the crossings that are open and the weeks the road is shut, and the chapter says in her mouth that this has never once been a number of days in anybody's mouth and is not going to be one this morning, which is the block's guard on the third frame.** The man of about fifty-eight with a badge says he cannot grant it and is told he was not being asked to, because no head in nine counties can grant it, and the road's own people are asked instead and are not in that county, and if they take it the count is theirs and is dated by whoever walked it and not by a gate, and if they will not then her line stays blank and a blank is a thing a page cannot read. **The woman of about fifty alone draws the distinction nobody else may draw: the road did not agree to be shut, the road agreed to be counted, those are not the same, and the page cannot tell them apart, which is the whole trouble with the page.** Nobody wrote it down and the gatekeeper read nothing out, because it was not on the paper. The man of about thirty-four with a sheet put one line on his own untitled sheet and did not show it to anybody, which is the fourth figure to go down that lane in a coat in about six weeks. **No name was spent, the road's people are not counted, no figure is printed for the road, and the two coda lists that changed went from thirteen to fifteen and from ten to eleven and were recounted clause by clause because a splitter cannot count them.** Nine of the ten chapters were not touched at all. Canon is `state/continuity.md` 1466 to 1472 and the hand-off is `state/open-threads.md` 454 to 464, of which item 464 is the list of what Batch 0003 must not do.
**Nobody thanked anybody in any of the ten chapters and nobody was thanked, the keeper of the room did not come down to the lane on two market mornings running and nobody agreed with the copyist about it either time, and the block ends with a man standing in a lane with a question he has correctly refused to answer, about nine people in a room who have eight questions and no findings, and a page in a satchel with three blanks in it that three of them do not know are three.**

## The review repair over Volume 13 Batch 0002, and the highest figures in this entry are now the review's

**A review of the repair pass above ran, returned eight findings, and its record is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0002.md` — the first review artifact Volume 13 has, and the reason that file exists is that the review's own output went to a gitignored log.** Five findings were applied and three are recorded and not applied, with reasons, in that file. **No plot beat, no dateline, no weekday, no money total, no name, no outline requirement and no ending moved, and no chapter was restarted.** The block's fifth beat, added by the repair pass above, was reviewed, found sound against `outline/volume-13.md` §8 Block 2, and stands.

**The three applied prose defects, and all three were in the chapter that pass had just rewritten and had certified clean.** **One, an impossible count printed on two consecutive days:** `chapter-0619.md` struck the page's tally as *about four of those three* refusals among three blank spaces, and `chapter-0620.md` repeated it. Both are now **two of those three**, with the unreached line kept, and the `about` goes with it because `outline/volume-13.md` §15 item 13 forbids `about` in front of a figure somebody in the scene could have counted. **The repair deliberately did NOT make the page show three refusals**, which is what §8 Block 2's phrase *three refusals and two signatures and one road that agrees* reads like on its own: that figure is the one the volume exists to dispute, and `chapter-0613.md` has a road-office man say *you have three refusals* and be told *I have three blanks and I cannot tell the difference, and neither can you, and that is the point*. **Correcting the page to three would have deleted the argument to satisfy the sentence that names its target.** **Two, a meta frame break, in two chapters and not one:** *a person in this manuscript* in `chapter-0619.md` is now *a person in nine counties*, and *the question of this book* in `chapter-0610.md` is now *the thing that all of it has been for*. The review found the first by searching for the string it had been given and missed the second, which is `volume-11/batch-0005.md`'s standing point arriving again — **a rule about register is not a rule about vocabulary, and a sweep that names its patterns keeps finding the one it named.** `chapter-0610.md` is nine words for nine words and so **Batch 0001's figures do not move.** **Three, and this is the one the review did not name:** the clause in `chapter-0619.md` claiming a tally of figures going down the lane in a coat, whose third listed item is a sheet written **the following day**. It was also the sentence the review flagged as exposition in place of an event. It is now the man declining the tally, in his own reasoning and in the terms the chapter has already given him — **what he wrote was a thing a person had asked for and not a number, and a man who has spent a month watching one figure get made out of another figure does not write the two in one line** — and **that makes `chapter-0620.md` correct as it already stood, because 0620 counts numbers and a request is not one. Renumbering 0619 to agree with 0620 would have destroyed a correct page to fix an impossible one.**

**The record finding is a false one, withdrawn, and it is recorded because a withdrawn figure belongs in the file that would have carried it.** The repair raised a finding of its own — that the recorded body series had been carried without a basis, and that **every prior instance of a non-reproducing figure in this project has been a wrong number whereas this one was a right number with no basis, which is worse because a successor would assume it was wrong and correct a correct figure** — and **it is false.** **The basis was written down in two places, `state/open-threads.md` 452 and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §80: *the scene body located structurally as everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda*.** Three candidate bases were tried against the pre-edit files and rejected, and the fourth was in the prompt the repair had just edited. This is `reviews/README.md`'s `volume-07/batch-0004.md` finding verbatim, and the rule is now on the record: **three bases rejected is not evidence of an unwritten basis, and the place to look for a missing basis before declaring one missing is the file you are already editing.** The two real defects that survive are small: **the name is misleading, because a body located as everything above the cost coda includes the title line and the dateline**, and **§80 states the two bases in one sentence that reads as though the title line were in both.** What the block actually had was **stale figures and not a missing basis**.

**The recount, and what it moved and what it did not.** Block **24,843**, per chapter 2,757 / 2,403 / 2,324 / 2,240 / 2,189 / 2,381 / 2,206 / 2,120 / 3,423 / 2,800, bodies 2,338 / 1,993 / 1,912 / 1,818 / 1,780 / 1,954 / 1,798 / 1,698 / 2,939 / 2,346, codas 408 to 484 at a mean share of 17.4 per cent over 14.1 to 19.9, `about` **671 at 27.01** per 1,000, Volume 13 to **45,176**, manuscript to **1,726,877**. **The money columns, the curly quotes at 290 and 290, the thank family at 33, the name budget and Volumes 01 to 12 did not move, and the 203-distinct collision set was NOT rebuilt, so it stands as measured and is not re-asserted as re-measured.** Canon is `state/continuity.md` 1473 to 1478 and the hand-off is `state/open-threads.md` 465 to 472, **of which item 472 is the list of what Batch 0003 must not do and it supersedes 464.**

**THE FIGURES IN THIS ENTRY ARE AS THEY STOOD WHEN THE BLOCK WAS MEASURED AND HAVE SINCE MOVED TWICE. This entry is the record of what the batch reported at the time and is not to be copied: the block is now 26,848, its per-chapter list is now 2,652 / 2,918 / 2,260 / 2,456 / 3,539 / 2,560 / 2,263 / 2,585 / 2,805 / 2,810, and the manuscript is 1,777,744. The current figures are in the review-fix section below and at `state/open-threads.md` 512.** ## Volume 13, Batch 0003 — Chapters 621 to 630, "The Road He Closed", days 1915 to 1956, 26,731 words in ten chapters

**The midpoint reversal, and it is the one the series row names.** A man who spent two blocks refusing to fill a head, refusing to answer a question of two words, and refusing to use the one true argument he has, is handed a night on which one of the six goes, and in about nine minutes in a room with about nine people in it he does the thing he has been right about three times, and the thing he does is a closure, and it is correct, and it costs somebody their isolation without asking them. **The people on that route are alive and continuing and the volume does not say so in a way anybody can use, and that refusal is built rather than asserted: the copyist tells a room that a good report and an absent report are not the same thing, and that the room's own four-and-a-half-year rule about derived series is the reason they look identical, and that a road which produces no returns can be shut for four years and nothing will arrive.**

**The ten days, derived off the anchor and not off the printed table.** 1915, 1916, 1921, 1923, 1924, 1925, 1926, 1928, 1949, 1956. Six market mornings, four not. Two firsts of a month, 1926 and 1956, and 1956 is the turn of the year. **The four non-market days are a Friday, a Sunday, a Monday and a Tuesday, so the room over the weigh-house was shut on every one of them and no chapter of this block put anything in that room on any of them** — the nine minutes happen in the hired room over a saddler's, which is shut on no day of the week, and the chapter says so.

**The five movements, and where they sit.** The works fail, at the second hour of the night on the twentieth, in Chapter 0621, and the failure is a scour at a joint and a newer scour with cut stone in it, and nobody is named. The arithmetic is checked and vindicated in the worst way available in Chapter 0622, and the man of about forty-seven says out loud that he got the number right and the order wrong, and nobody thanks him. **The recovery beat is Chapter 0623 and it is the volume's ordinary work and the volume's catastrophe in the same three courses of lime.** The question is asked a second time at a counter in Chapter 0624 and is not answered. The nine minutes are Chapter 0625. The road goes quiet with no arrangement in Chapter 0626, and the reason there is no arrangement is the room's own correct standing, which the copyist says out loud for the first time in four and a half years: **there is no list of keepers in nine counties and therefore no address for anybody.** The first of a month is Chapter 0627, the arithmetic paid and not enjoyed, and the pressure named: the fifth time there will not be a gap. She finds out in a market in Chapter 0628, from a man buying a basket, four days late, and what she says is not a complaint. The cost is Chapter 0629 and it is not that anybody is hurt, and the count belongs to nobody. The year turns in Chapter 0630 and he answers a question nobody asked.

**What is new in this block and was not on the page before.** One, **there is no address**, said out loud by the copyist as a consequence of a standing the volume has treated as a virtue for four and a half years, and she does not take the standing back because the standing is right. Two, **nobody being hurt is the answer a man gives when he has not found the cost**, and the only cost three weeks can find is that it cost a woman the only thing she has, which is not being asked. Three, **the middle course** — the one with nothing on it that will fall and nothing under it that will hold it — is named in a mason's mouth four days off a market town, and it is the culvert at the gate and the new sill and the seventh line, and the volume puts the same object in all three places and says so in no document. Four, **it has happened three times** and it is the first of the three in thirty-one years that anybody acted on. Five, **Hester Vane**, the one name the block spent, named for the first time by herself and refused three times to give a count in a room.

**The instrument that cost the most, and it is worth a successor's time.** The coda's three figure lists are hand-checkable and not machine-checkable, because a list separator and a number join are the same three letters: *a hundred and four*, *a foot and a half*, *four and a half years*, *three series and none of them made out of another* all contain **and**. Two counters were written and both were wrong in opposite directions before a third distinguished a number join from a list joiner, and with that rule all thirty lists foot. Three genuine ambiguities were reworded on the page rather than argued. **And the block was written with zero question marks, which was a real defect, and twenty were put in across seven chapters, and the two chapters that finished with none were the two that had gained a beat after the sweep was run — which is the standing finding at `state/continuity.md` 1478 in a new dress: a pass that verifies every instrument against a file has not read that file, and a beat added after a sweep is unswept.**

**The counters, recounted on the files, with both bases named separately because they are not the same.** **File total, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own, the ten added: 26,844, per chapter 2,652 / 2,918 / 2,260 / 2,456 / 3,539 / 2,560 / 2,263 / 2,582 / 2,804 / 2,810, and every one of those figures is recounted after the review repair and not carried forward from the first printing.** **Scene body, located as everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which is the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph and which THEREFORE INCLUDES THE TITLE LINE AND THE DATELINE: 2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,011 / 2,188 / 2,207, min 1,756 at 0627 and max 2,970 at 0625.** Codas 482 / 500 / 481 / 476 / 569 / 519 / 507 / 571 / 616 / 603, so 476 to 616, at a mean share of 20.03 per cent over 16.08 to 22.4, and the share is reported as a measured consequence of body length and not as a virtue. `about` 603 at 22.46 per 1,000. Thank family 40, every one a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked, all forty read in context. Curly doubles 284 and 284, 0 straight doubles, 0 curly apostrophes, 45 straight apostrophes, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 20 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 0 semicolons. `Aren Kest` 13 / 3 / 2 / 3 / 10 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 1 / 4 and in every chapter; `Mara Vey` 0. Duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, against the six hundred and twenty before and against themselves, 0 and 0. **Longest CHAIN of consecutive shared eight-word windows against the six hundred and twenty before: 36 windows, which is forty-three words, at `chapter-0624.md` in the cost coda's own person list; then 29 at 0625, 25 at 0629, 25 at 0625, 24 at 0623, 23 at 0628, 23 at 0625, 22 at 0628. THE THIRTY-SIX IS A CORPUS MEASURE AND NOT A FILE-PAIR RUN, and the first printing of this entry called it *36, at 0624 against 0604* and both halves of that are wrong: the thirty-six chains across fourteen of the six hundred and twenty prior files and no single one of them carries it, `chapter-0624.md` against `chapter-0604.md` is a longest contiguous word-run of **24** on the same whitespace token, and the largest partner any one file carries is nineteen windows, which is twenty-five words, against `chapter-0609.md`. The volume record before this block was 42 on the chain basis.** The twenty-five barred terms are at 0 across all ten files. **The collision set was rebuilt with three extractors over the six hundred and twenty chapters before this block and they returned 470 instances / 91 distinct, 75 / 72 and 131 / 120, a union of 676 instances and 208 distinct, and the four barred numerals leave a free set of 207 out of the 208, which is printed because a collision report without it is no result. The ten columns are 209 / 221 / 233 / 241 / **253** / 268 / 279 / 296 / 318 / 341, ten distinct, 9 to 11 items a column, each footing three ways, and colliding with that set zero times. **252 was printed by this block and is gone: two hundred and fifty-two spells the barred numeral fifty-two inside the number itself, and the column at 0625 was re-ordered by moving one item price from two pence to three so that it foots at two hundred and fifty-three, and the printed total was moved only because the items moved.** The 203 printed for Batch 0002 was not rebuilt and is not re-asserted; 203 and 208 were built by different extractors over different chapter counts and the difference is not a discrepancy.**

**Manuscript.** Volumes 01 to 12 recount to 1,681,701 in six hundred chapters and do not move; Batch 0001 is 20,333; Batch 0002 is 24,843; Batch 0003 is 26,844; **Volume 13 is 72,020 in thirty chapters and the manuscript is 1,753,721 in six hundred and thirty**, and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 + 26,844 reproduces it. Canon is `state/continuity.md` 1479 to 1494 and the hand-off is `state/open-threads.md` 473 to 487, **of which 487 is the list of what Batch 0004 must not do.** `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` is the successor's brief and is the only prompt created by this phase.

**The review repair on this block, and what it changed, with every figure that moved printed beside the figure it replaced.** A review of `novel: save writer work batch-0003` raised thirteen findings and marked six things checked and not defects. This entry records what was repaired and what was not, and it does not change a line of plot. **Three findings were contradictions inside the block's own central arithmetic and all three had been copied into `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, which is why they are the three that mattered.**

**One, the works' capacity was printed as both seven and six.** `chapter-0607.md`, `chapter-0621.md` and `chapter-0622.md` all say the works will take seven and has taken seven, and `chapter-0622.md` already holds the two-level reconciliation in the sentence *There is one thing the works will not take and the thing it will not take has to come off*. Against that, `chapter-0625.md` said **will take six and there are seven on it**, `chapter-0627.md` said **it will take six, and it took six before anybody in this county was born** using the same rhetorical construction and the same *before anybody in this county* pivot as 0622, `chapter-0630.md` had Slade say **it will take six** and the keeper of the room say **The seven is still seven** in one unmediated scene, and `chapter-0621.md` said the third went because the works **is carrying more than it will take** without saying what the more than was. **CANON IS SEVEN, and there was never a case for six: the works will take seven, seven roads go through it, seven are on it, and the seventh is the one thing on it that the works will not take because it has no figure, no name and nobody whose job it is, and taking it off leaves six, which is inside what the works will take.** Repaired: 0621 now says what the more than is, 0625's speech says *a set of works that will take seven and there are seven on it, and there is one thing on it the works will not take*, 0625's consequence sentence says *six is inside what it will take*, 0627 says *it will take seven, and it took seven before anybody in this county was born*, 0630's Slade says *it will take seven*, and the keeper of the room's **The seven is still seven** became **The seventh is still off the works**, which is a statement about the status of a line and not a second claim about a capacity.

**Two, Chapter 0628 could not happen as written.** The closure is the second hour of the night on Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth and the chapter is Goatmonth the third, four days later, and three lines put Nell Craddock's knowledge and her two visits up the slope **since Friday**, which is Hearthmonth the twenty-seventh, two days before the closure, and a fourth put the copyist's thinking there as well, and the clerk opened by saying **two days and a bit** for an interval of four. The concealment beat, twice saying no to the woman at the top of the slope, only works if she already held something, and what she can hold without anybody having told her anything is a belief. **Repaired on that footing and no other.** The clerk says **Four days**, the slope is **twice up there since Monday**, her account of it is **I had not been told a thing and I said no on the strength of a woman of about sixty asking me twice**, the second distinction she draws is **I have known since Monday that something had come off those works, and I have not been told**, the copyist has been thinking about the consequence **since Sunday night**, and the word she has been looking for she has been looking for **since Monday**. **Four days of carting is kept**, the cost coda's lie told twice is now a lie the page has actually committed, and no date in the chapter is earlier than the closure.

**Three, Chapter 0629's day count was short by four.** Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth is day 1924 and Goatmonth the twenty-fourth is day 1949, and the interval is twenty-five days, and the chapter printed **twenty-one days** in its second sentence and **three weeks** in five places and **nine days ago** in the badge-man's account of the third of the three. All eight are now **twenty-five days**, in the narration, in the speech, in the cost line, in the coda's line and in the coda's own time list, which still foots at fourteen figures.

**Four, `chapter-0630.md` said four households.** The figure is nine: *called Nell Craddock by about nine households* at 0606, *about nine houses at the top end and about nine at the bottom* at 0609, *about nine houses at one end and about nine at the other* at 0625. Now **about nine households on a road**.

**Five, `chapter-0626.md` said the machinery was ready nine hours after it was refused.** The refusal is at two in the morning on the night of the twenty-ninth and the machinery is ready at seven the same morning, which is **about five hours**, and the same speech said two people had told him **in two days** when the second of them spoke at the counter that morning, so it is now **inside one day**. The coda's time list carries **about five hours** in place of about nine hours and still foots at eleven.

**Six, four state sentences printed Volume 13 as 71,897 and each contradicted its own arithmetic,** because 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 + 26,731 is 71,907. Corrected on the first pass of this repair and then superseded again by the recounts below.

**Seven, `state/open-threads.md` 473 put day 1998 at the seventeenth of Mudmonth.** 1986 is the first of Mudmonth YR 321 and 1998 is the **thirteenth**, which is what `batch-0004/PROMPT.md` and `state/current.md` already said. The same sentence then said **The month starts inside Block 0004 are 1986 and 2016 and 1986 is inside it and 2016 is outside it**, which is a set that cannot be inside a block that one of its members is outside. Block 0004 has **one** month start inside it, at 1986, and 2016 is a month start that is outside it.

**Eight, the thirty-six-word run was attributed to a file pair it does not belong to.** Recorded above and in `state/continuity.md` 1495 and corrected in `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.

**Nine, `chapter-0622.md`'s badge-man opened on two spans with no connective**, *Thirty-one years* followed by *I have put four and a half years of other people's figures into rooms*. Now **And for four and a half of them I have been putting other men's figures into that room**, which keeps both spans, gives the connective and is the construction 0626 uses correctly.

**Ten, `chapter-0624.md`'s second clause had no verb**, *I am telling you I heard it and not that I have come to be told*, which is not the volume's own move. Now **I am saying I heard it and I am not saying I have come here to be told**.

**Eleven, an unrecorded body echo.** `chapter-0626.md` and `chapter-0628.md` share a **twenty-one word** span almost whole with one clause swapped: *and the other five I could not put anywhere, and I have never once thought about the five before, and I*. It sits under the block's twenty-two word line, so the sweep did not name it, and it is the same man making the same argument with the same figure, so it is an echo and not a lift. It is now recorded in `state/continuity.md` 1495 and it survives the repair above, because 0626 was not re-voiced and 0628's echo is in a different line.

**Twelve, `state/current.md`'s item thirteen read as an exhaustive list of the chapters in which Aren Kest is in the lane.** He is in the lane in 0626 as well, walking past the weigh-house at the fourth hour, and he is about forty paces off in a market street in 0628. All six are now named.

**Thirteen, three cost codas listed persons who are not in the chapter without saying so.** 0623 listed the copyist, the man of about forty-seven with a page and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room; 0625 listed the woman of about forty who runs a lime kiln; 0628 listed the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room. **This is the house convention and not a slip** — 0625 already ends its list *a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room and was not there* — so the repair is the convention applied and not the convention replaced, and each of the three now carries the absence the others were already carrying. **A fourteenth instance was found by reading and not by the review: 0625's coda closed *the fourteenth of them was not in the room and has not been told and will find out in a market*, and the person who will find it out in a market is the eleventh and not the fourteenth. Corrected, and the fourteenth now says only that she was not in that room.**

**Fourteen, and this one the review certified as clean and it was not.** Chapter 0625's money column totalled **two hundred and fifty-two pence**, and that spelling contains the barred numeral `fifty-two` inside the number itself, which is the defect `state/continuity.md` 1336 repaired in an earlier volume and the defect this volume's own prompt forbids in *every total, over-figure and printed price*. The review listed all twenty-five barred terms at 0 across the ten files and was wrong; a whitespace-token whole-word sweep finds it at 2. **The column was re-ordered by moving one item price from two pence to three and the printed total moved to two hundred and fifty-three as a consequence and not as a correction, which is the rule this project has printed twice: for a barred figure, change the column and never the printed total.** Two hundred and fifty-three is not in the six hundred and twenty chapters before this block, and the mark conversion is five marks and thirteen over, which is clean. Six closed-volume files also print a total whose spelling contains a barred numeral — 0117, 0133, 0138, 0141 and 0143 in Volume 03 and 0401 in Volume 09 — **and they are recorded here and not repaired, because a closed volume is not repaired on a hunch.**

**Nothing in this repair changed the plot, a dateline, a chapter's beat, a character's motive or the planned ending, and no chapter was restarted.** The block's ten days are the same ten days, the year still turns on the last of them, the register still stands at nine questions with no finding under any of them, the seventh line is still off the works and no road is reopened, and the ending of the volume is where `outline/volume-13.md` puts it.

## Volume 13, Batch 0004 — Chapters 631 to 640, "What the Room Owes a Road"

**Days 1957 to 1998 is the block and this block wrote days 1958, 1961, 1965, 1970, 1972, 1973, 1976, 1977, 1978 and 1986 out of it. Every day of the block is YR 321. 23,719 words in ten chapters, counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included and the ten added, per chapter 2,573 / 2,595 / 2,638 / 2,268 / 2,363 / 2,177 / 2,411 / 2,237 / 2,206 / 2,251. On the other basis, the scene body located structurally as everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which therefore includes the title line and the dateline and is not in fact a scene body, the figures are 1,992 / 2,064 / 2,090 / 1,773 / 1,861 / 1,637 / 1,893 / 1,715 / 1,683 / 1,702. Codas 581 / 531 / 548 / 495 / 502 / 540 / 518 / 522 / 523 / 549 and a mean share of 22.46 per cent over 20.46 to 24.80, and this block held the coda short and let the share rise, which is the opposite branch to Batch 0003's and is reported as a measured consequence of body length and not as a virtue. **THE MANUSCRIPT FIGURE IN THIS SENTENCE MOVED TWICE AFTER IT WAS WRITTEN AND THE CORRECTION IS ON THE PAGE: the block is 23,719 and Volumes 01 to 12 are 1,681,701 and unmoved, but Batch 0003 is 26,848 and not 26,844, because the review repair over Batch 0003 added four words to `chapter-0629.md` and `chapter-0628.md` after this entry was written. The manuscript is 1,777,744 in six hundred and forty chapters and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 + 26,848 + 23,719 reproduces it, per file and concatenated.**

**What the block is, in one sentence: a room that has spent four and a half years proving that it cannot hold a decision is asked by nine people to hold a rule instead, and the rule they argue into existence over nine days is the only thing anybody has won in this volume, and the cost of it is that a road nobody can write to is still not covered by it, and that a road went quiet during the nine days and is not got back.**

**The five beats, and where each of them is.** Chapter 0631, day 1958, a Saturday and a market morning, has the people come: the badge-man has no head with which to call a meeting and says so in the market street to nobody in particular and about nine people hear and four care, the keeper of the room refuses to have it opened on a non-market day and refuses to be handed a second register entry, the copyist sets her condition, five of the seven roads send a person, a man of about fifty-two arrives who keeps a road and will not name it, and two roads send writing instead of a person. Chapter 0632, day 1961, a Tuesday with the room shut, sets the argument off the past, and the man of about forty-seven is the hardest person to argue with because he is right and says so. Chapter 0633, day 1965, a Saturday, reads the two letters out loud with the door open and gets the first clause out of them. Chapter 0634, day 1970, a Thursday, is the first of nine days and gets the witness clause out of Hester Vane over four days. Chapter 0635, day 1972, a Saturday, is the third of nine and gets the burden clause out of the man of about thirty-one who wants it in, and the man of about fifty-two goes home. Chapter 0636, day 1973, a Sunday, is the recovery, at a bridge a day and a half off, six men and a cart nobody sent, three courses laid and a fourth refused for a reason nobody has ever asked the man of about fifty for. Chapter 0637, day 1976, a Wednesday in the open, has the man of about thirty-one refuse to price the burden, the man of about forty-seven right for the fifth time about a letter to a keeper, the empty chair, and the badge-man refusing to ask a second time. Chapter 0638, day 1977, a Thursday, is the eighth of nine, finishes the rule in nine hands with no copy, and has the woman of about thirty-six name what the rule does not cover, and then a carter says nothing has come from the end of it. Chapter 0639, day 1978, a Friday, is the ninth of nine, tries for two hours to find the road and cannot, and hands out nine pieces of paper. Chapter 0640, day 1986, Mudmonth the first, a Saturday and a market morning and the block's only first of a month, is the hearing in the street, an empty stair, Berta Lomax reading the three clauses aloud, and a road that is here and unknown.

**The rule, in its finished form, in three clauses.** No road is shut until the man who proposes to shut it has been to the end of that road and has said it out loud, in words that road uses, in a place where somebody who works that road can hear him, and on a day that road is being used. No road is shut without a witness standing on that road at the time, named by the people who work that road and not by the man proposing to shut it, and if that witness cannot be found afterwards then the road is open. The burden of showing that a road may be shut lies with the man who says it may be shut, and if that man cannot be named and found afterwards then nothing has been shown and the road is open. **There is no copy. Nine hands, and the copyist refused to hold a tenth, and the room's own answer to where a rule goes is nine hands.**

**The imperfection, named by somebody who is not the protagonist: it covers roads that can be written to.** A woman of about thirty-six who keeps a road in the county where a list of names was burned said it on the eighth of the nine days and gave the reason that the men who burned the list in her county did not hate anybody and that it was a sheet, and that a sheet with good manners on it is still a sheet. Nobody argued with her.

**The cost, and it is not tidied.** A man of about fifty-two who would not name his road went home on the third of nine days saying he would be back on the ninth, was not there on the seventh or the ninth, and on the eighth a carter said nothing had come from the end of it since the middle of the week before. Nobody in nine counties knows which road it is. The copyist did not write it down and says she would not have written it down anyway, because the only thing anybody would have about that road would be a line, and a line is a thing you can put beside another line. The badge-man asked once and did not ask twice. At the close of the block nothing has come from that road, no clause of the rule reaches it, and Chapter 0640 says so and says that no chapter after that one is going to say which road it was.

**The recovery beat, which is the volume's ordinary work and its catastrophe in the same masonry.** A bridge a day and a half off, six men, a boy of about seventeen and a cart nobody sent, a carter's boy who paid for the cart out of his own wage, and the man of about fifty who lays stone clearing it down to the stone, laying three courses in lime and refusing the fourth for a reason nobody has asked him for in thirty years: in thirty years a man will come with a bar and find courses, and if there are three he can ask the men who were here and about four will be dead, and if there are four there is one nobody can account for and that is the one that will go, and a rule about a person who can be named and found is not a rule about a course of stone, and somebody in nine counties ought to write one down. **Aren Kest was four days off at the works, heard about it the next morning from a carter buying a basket, and did not go and look, and nobody told the bridge-man either, and the chapter says those two facts are the whole of what happened between them in about four and a half years and neither is in any document.**

**The instruments, all recounted on the ten files.** Money columns at 246 / 272 / 291 / 304 / 259 / 238 / 317 / 285 / 274 / 348 pence, ten distinct, 10 to 12 items a column, each footing three ways. The prior set was rebuilt over the six hundred and thirty chapters before this block with three extractors, which returned 4,082 instances / 208 distinct, 355 / 41 and 218 / 47; the union is 208 distinct and the four barred numerals leave a free set of 207 out of the 208, printed here because a collision report without it is no result. **REC recounted on the files by the verification pass over this block: the same three extractors, keyed on both the digit and the spelled price and over the same six hundred and thirty chapters, returned 133 instances / 100 distinct, 150 / 141 and 137 / 126, a union of 190 distinct and a free set of 188 out of the 190. **THE EXTRACTOR IS DEFINED HERE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS REPOSITORY, AND IT IS DEFINED BECAUSE NO FILE EVER DEFINED IT, which is why neither this set nor the one above it could be reproduced by anyone who tried, including the pass that printed it. Scope: the last two paragraphs of each of the six hundred and thirty chapters before this block. Extractor one, the printed total, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence.` and parsed to an integer, returns 194 instances and 168 distinct. Extractor two, the restated total inside the house conversion `and <spelled number> pence is <spelled number> marks and <spelled number> over`, returns 97 instances and 92 distinct, and is a strict subset of extractor one. The union of the two is 168 distinct, the four barred numerals appear nowhere in it, so the free set is 168 out of the 168 and the barred-numeral rule removes nothing from it. The ten columns of this block collide with that set zero times. Note the scope: only 214 of the six hundred and thirty prior chapters carry a printed total at all, because the coda form begins in Volume 09 and is universal from Volume 10, so no earlier volume can collide with anything.** **Collision: zero of ten.** No total, over-figure or printed price spells thirty-six, fifty-two, sixty-one or sixty-six. Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, against the six hundred and thirty before and against themselves, 0 and 0. **On a whitespace-token normaliser with punctuation kept, sliding an eight-word window one token at a time, the longest chain of consecutive shared eight-word windows against the six hundred and thirty prior chapters is 24 windows, which is thirty-one words, at `chapter-0631.md`, and it is a CORPUS chain and not a file-pair run, and it chains across 236 of the six hundred and thirty prior files and no single one of them carries it; the largest chain any one new file holds against any one prior file is 19 windows, which is twenty-six words, and six pairs tie at it and no one pair owns it: 0631 against 0613, 0634 against 0605, 0634 against 0615, 0634 against 0623, 0635 against 0624, and 0640 against 0613. **The 25 and 20 that a review-fix pass printed in place of these are the same two chains counted one window high, not a re-measurement, and both are reverted here; all four figures were re-run on the ten files after that pass and none of them moved.** The first draft of the coda person lists ran to 68 consecutive shared windows, longer than any figure in this volume, and the repair was to reorder nine of the ten lists and reword six items.

**The name budget: 4 of 5 with one slot unspent.** Batch 0004 spent one, `Berta Lomax`, 34, of the fruit valley, who is the reader and is the woman who reads the clause about the right to refuse aloud as the volume's last line, and Batch 0005 has one name and not two. Nobody thanked anybody in any of the ten chapters, in this file, or in the state files this block wrote. Canon is `state/continuity.md` 1496 to 1503 and the hand-off is `state/open-threads.md` 489 to 503, of which item 503 is the list of what Batch 0005 must not do.

The current figures are in the review-fix section below and at `state/continuity.md` 1510.** ## Volume 13, Batch 0003 verification pass — no chapter written, two sentences repaired, and the prompt was stale

**The prompt handed to this pass was `workspace/volume-13/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` and it instructs the writer to produce Chapters 621 to 630. Those ten chapters were already finished prose on disk, and Batch 0004's ten chapters stood complete behind them, and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` already existed.** Its own first instruction is to count the chapter files and believe the count over the prompt, and the count is **forty files and `chapter-0640.md`**. **Nothing was written, no chapter was rewritten, no marker was written, no controller file was touched, and no prompt was created — a second copy of Batch 0005's brief is the fourteenth instance of the hazard this project keeps meeting.** Canon at `state/continuity.md` 1504 to 1508; hand-off at `state/open-threads.md` 504 to 510, of which 510 is the list of what the next pass must not do.

**What the verification found.** Every instrument re-run on the ten files and reproduced: **26,844 words in ten chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the ten added, and a concatenated `cat` returns the same figure**; scene bodies 2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,011 / 2,188 / 2,207 against the volume's 1,161 floor; codas 476 to 616 at a mean share of 20.03 per cent; `about` 603 at 22.46 per 1,000; the thank family 40 and every one a negation, all read in context; curly doubles 284 and 284 with no straight doubles and no curly apostrophes; no em dash, en dash, exclamation mark or semicolon; 20 question marks; bold parity even on every line; all twenty-five barred terms and all four barred numerals at 0; `Mara Vey` at 0, `Aren Kest` 42 in ten of ten, name budget 3 of 5; exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, 0 and 0; **all ten money columns footing four ways at 209 / 221 / 233 / 241 / 253 / 268 / 279 / 296 / 318 / 341, ten distinct, 9 to 11 items a column, no barred numeral in any total, over-figure or printed price; and all thirty coda figure lists counted and footing.**

**What the verification repaired, and it is two sentences and one class.** `chapter-0627.md` had the man of about fifty-eight with a badge say *it is the whole of the block* and `chapter-0628.md` had the narration say *it is the sentence this block is built on*. **In both the word is the production unit and not a thing in the world**, which is the meta-frame class `state/continuity.md` 1478 named after this volume had already paid for it twice with *this manuscript* and *this book*. The badge-man now says *the whole of the answer*, which is the sentence he is actually answering, and the narration now says *it was the sentence she had come up the lane to say*, which keeps the beat and drops the narrator's reference to its own batch. Neither repair touches a figure, a date, a name or a beat, and the block moves three words, all of them in 0628.

**Three false DEFECTS this pass produced against correct pages, which is the finding worth having.** A prefix glob, `chapter-062*.md`, matches 0620 to 0629 and silently pulls in the neighbouring batch's last chapter, which made the block eleven files and 29,644 words. A comma splitter cannot count the coda figure lists, because a list separator and a number join are the same three letters and the persons lists separate on `and` while the distance and time lists separate on commas. And a leftmost-match regex on `and (.+?) are ... figures of` starts at the wrong `and` and reported 33 items against a printed fourteen in nine chapters running. **In all three cases the page was right and the counter was wrong, and the only thing that caught it was reading the file.**

**One existing record does not reproduce and it is about duplication.** The block's longest run against the prior twenty chapters is 36 words and both this file and `state/continuity.md` 1503 place it **in the cost coda's own person list**. It is **scene prose, `chapter-0625.md` against `chapter-0620.md`** — the same man refusing the same request for the same reason five days apart, a deliberate recurrence. **A successor must re-derive that category before acting on it, and must not repair the recurrence on the strength of the record's label.**

**The manuscript after this pass is 1,777,743 in six hundred and forty chapters**, per file and concatenated, the two bases agreeing. **Volumes 01 to 12 are 1,681,701 in six hundred and do not move; Volume 13's four blocks are 20,333 / 24,843 / 26,847 / 23,719 and Volume 13 is 95,742 in forty.** The 26,844 this file carried for Batch 0003 was correct before this pass and is 26,847 after it.

## Volume 13, Batch 0003 review-fix pass — no chapter written, one sentence repaired in 0629, three stale figures corrected, and the review's findings put in `reviews/`

**The reviewed artifact is commit `b2500e2` *novel: save writer work batch-0003*, a verification-and-repair pass over Chapters 621 to 630 that correctly refused a stale prompt, repaired two meta-frame sentences, and rewrote only the state records. The review of it returned three findings and one minor note from `logs/batch-0003.review.log`, which is gitignored, and the durable record of both is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0003.md`. This section is the state's half of the disposition.**

**WHAT WAS REPAIRED IN THE PROSE, AND IT IS ONE SENTENCE IN ONE FILE.** `chapters/volume-13/chapter-0629.md` line 59 read *it is the thing Block 0004 stands on*: the production unit's own numbered label, printed in scene prose, in a chapter the reviewed pass had certified **2,804 words, unchanged**. It now reads **and it is the thing the thirty-one years stand on**, which is nine words for eight and points the narration at the badge-man's speech two paragraphs below, in which he says this is the first of three occasions in thirty-one years on which a man who could see a fault acted on it. **Nothing else in 0629 was touched, no dateline, no weekday, no money figure, no name and no beat, and the file is 2,805 words.**

**WHAT WAS REPAIRED IN THE RECORDS, AND IT IS THREE FIGURES IN THREE PLACES THAT HAD NO MARKER.** `state/current.md` line 16, `state/open-threads.md` 502 and `state/continuity.md` 1503 all still asserted **26,844 / 95,739 / 1,777,440** in the present tense after the reviewed pass had corrected the same figures elsewhere and declared them superseded in one place only. **The worst of the three is 502, which sits directly above item 503, the binding list of what Batch 0005 must not do**, so the operative brief for the next writer carried three wrong numbers with nothing to signal it. All three are corrected on the page with a marker in the sentence, and 1503 and 505 are marked falsified in place rather than deleted, so the record of what was claimed survives beside the record of what is true.

**WHAT WAS RECORDED AND NOT ACTED ON, AND WHY THAT IS THE RIGHT ANSWER RATHER THAN A DELAY.** The two known instances in closed Batch 0002 were correctly declined on scope and correctly identified by the review as having no owner. **The owner is now written down at `state/open-threads.md` 511: a dedicated repair dispatch with the whole manuscript open, minimum scope the two Volume 13 instances, and the three closed-volume instances taken in the same pass.** It is not Batch 0005, because `workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` bars writing 601 to 640 and item 503 bars repairing closed prose on a hunch, and **a wrong prohibition is obeyed more reliably than a right instruction, so neither bar was touched and neither was argued with.** The inventory was re-derived rather than inherited and **the class is EIGHT, not two: the reviewed pass missed one inside its own block, and a manuscript-wide sweep carried this pass found four that neither pass had reported, in Volumes 12, 05 and 01. Two further candidates were checked by reading them and are in-world, and are recorded so a successor does not spend an afternoon on them.**

**THE RECOUNT, EVERY BASIS NAMED, AND IT IS THE FIGURE SET AT `state/continuity.md` 1510.** Batch 0003 is **26,848** in ten chapters, per chapter **2,652 / 2,918 / 2,260 / 2,456 / 3,539 / 2,560 / 2,263 / 2,585 / 2,805 / 2,810**, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the ten added, with a concatenated `cat` of the ten returning the same figure and no file missing its trailing newline. **Volume 13 is 95,743 in forty chapters and the manuscript is 1,777,744 in six hundred and forty, Volumes 01 to 12 unmoved at 1,681,701.** Bodies, which include the title line and the dateline, are **2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,014 / 2,189 / 2,207**; codas are **482 / 500 / 481 / 476 / 569 / 519 / 507 / 571 / 616 / 603**; the mean coda share is **20.02** per cent over 16.08 to 22.4. **`about` IS 603 at 22.46 per 1,000 and did not move.** Curly doubles 284 and 284, straight doubles 0, curly apostrophes 0, straight apostrophes 45, em dashes 0, en dashes 0, question marks 20, exclamation marks 0, semicolons 0, bold parity even throughout, duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more 0. **Chapter 0629's column was footed separately because it is the file that was touched: 318 pence, six marks and thirty over at forty-eight a mark, as printed.**

**ONE RECORD OF THE REVIEWED PASS DOES NOT REPRODUCE, AND THE CORRECTION IT APPLIED DOES NOT REPRODUCE EITHER.** That pass recorded the block's longest run against the six hundred and twenty prior chapters as 36 words and corrected the label of an earlier record that had placed it in a cost coda's person list. **Re-run on the basis it names, the maximum is 43 words and it is the cost coda's recited frame at `chapter-0624.md`**, with the 0625/0620 scene recurrence second at 36; on a second basis, words rather than whitespace tokens with the title line dropped, only 0623 moves. **The advice survives and is unchanged: the run at 0625 and 0620 is a deliberate scene recurrence and must not be repaired.** What does not survive is the confidence that the label was wrong, and the lesson is the one this project has now stated four times, that **a figure about a sweep must carry its basis, and the correction of such a figure is subject to the same rule as the figure.**

**NOTHING IN THE STORY MOVED.** The works, the failure, the nine minutes, the closure, the missing address, the four days in a market, the middle course, the cost that is not harm, the turn of the year and *I wanted it* all stand exactly as Batch 0003 left them. **The whole of the manuscript change across the two passes is four words in three sentences of one class.** No prompt was created, no marker was written, no controller file was touched, `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `phase-000-bootstrap`, `planned`, and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` was not created, not replaced and not duplicated.
