## Volume 13, Batch 0005 — Chapters 641 to 650, "Two Beds, One Piece of Work", days 1999 to 2040, 23,337 words in ten chapters, written in one pass and then measured and repaired four times

**The last block of Volume 13, and day 2040 is the volume's last day.** Ten finished chapters, 601 to 640 untouched, no repair of anything in Volumes 01 to 12, no marker of any kind, and exactly one record created afterwards, `outline/volumes/volume-13-close.md`. **The prompt was checked before anything was drafted, as its own last instruction requires: `ls chapters/volume-13/ | wc -l` returned forty and `ls chapters/volume-13/ | sort | tail -1` returned `chapter-0640.md` before this block was written, and the prompt exists and is Batch 0005's brief and was not duplicated.**

### The calendar, derived and not copied, and every one of the ten datelines reconciles

Off the single anchor of **day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday**, on the carried thirty-day month, with the day-of-month half and the weekday half each derived from the day number and never from one another, and with Thawmonth the first of YR 321 at day 1956 and Mudmonth the first of YR 321 at day 1986 and Greenmonth the first of YR 321 at day 2016. Block 0005 is **days 1999 to 2040 inclusive, forty-two days, the fourteenth of Mudmonth YR 321 to the twenty-fifth of Greenmonth YR 321, and every day of it is YR 321.** **The ten days are 2000, 2005, 2007, 2012, 2016, 2019, 2022, 2026, 2028 and 2040, and their weekdays are Saturday, Thursday, Saturday, Thursday, Monday, Thursday, Sunday, Thursday, Saturday and Thursday.** **Eight of the ten are market mornings and the two that are not are a Monday and a Sunday, and the room over the weigh-house was shut on both of those and each of those two chapters says so on the page and puts the whole of its argument in the street or in a market street with about four carts in it.** **2016 is the block's only first of a month, a Monday, and not a market morning, and it carries Chapter 0645, on which the badge-man says the hearing stands in the street and says the three clauses aloud in a street for the first time.** 2040 is the twenty-fifth of Greenmonth, a Thursday, a market morning, and the volume's last day, and it carries Chapter 0650. **The printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was not read for the derivation and its error stands as recorded: it prints 2016 on a Wednesday where the chain gives a Monday, and 1986 on a Friday where the chain gives a Saturday, and it is a certificate and not an authority.**

### The rule, the imperfection, the answer, and what was not done

**The three clauses are not re-opened, not softened and not assembled, and no chapter of this block prints a single copy of them or lets anybody assemble one.** The meeting of 2007 decides in about four minutes to write nothing at all, and the copyist's reason is the reason the clauses went into nine hands. **The imperfection is named twice more in the open, in Chapter 0645 in a market street to about nine people who keep no road and in Chapter 0648 in about four minutes, and no chapter calls it small and no chapter reaches a road that cannot be written to.** **The question left open on the fourth of the nine days is answered in Chapter 0648, in about four minutes, in a market street, and it is not softened: the badge-man says yes, the right to refuse is dangerous, and the danger is not that a road says no but that in about four months a man will be able to say the road refused and nobody will ever be able to reach inside the refusal to find out whether the road refused or was asked in a way it could not say no to, and he says out loud that he wanted to put something in a rule about it and is not going to.** The finding he states aloud afterwards is that they have thought about it and it is worse and not better. **The clause about the right to refuse exists in nine hands and in nobody's keeping, it is read aloud in Chapter 0650 in a room with about nine people in it, and nobody thanks her and nobody thanks anybody anywhere in the block.** **The road that went quiet is not got back, is not named, is not visited, is not sent to, is not written about by anybody who knows, and is not resolved in either direction including by being called unimportant; the woman of about thirty-four who came four days for one sentence asks for its name in a market street in Chapter 0643 and is refused, and says she will say her sentence on the first of every month, and the badge-man says in Chapter 0649 that nine people knowing a thing is the whole of what nine people knowing a thing amounts to.**

### The separation, the recovery, and the last page

**The separation is Chapter 0644, in a lane at the end of a walk, in about nine exchanges, without a scene, and neither of them says the word and both mean it and the word is not printed anywhere in the block.** She says three things and one of them is that there is no document, and he says the one thing that is his. The work continues, the two of them keep separate allies and the right to disagree, and **the four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them, and nothing between them is described in any document, and the two of them are in one room together with a third person in it in none of the ten chapters.** **The recovery beat is Chapter 0646, the volume's ordinary work in the same masonry as its catastrophe: a drain at the town's own gate, three courses of sill laid in lime, a fourth not laid, a cart nobody sent, a boy's labour from before light, and a wage counted out loud from nine households at the boards.** The mason's reason is new and is about lime and a winter and not about a course nobody can account for, and the stone for the fourth course is laid out in a line on the bank with the mallet on it. **The volume's last main-world page is a hearing with no bench in it, in a room over a saddler's and not in the room over the weigh-house and for a stated reason, with the fourth head ruled and empty, a register of nine questions with no finding under any of them, a floor washed by somebody nobody knew, a half leaf face down on a table, a rule in fragments, a woman of about twenty-nine reading the standing of the hearing aloud, and then Berta Lomax of the fruit valley reading the clause about the right to refuse, and nobody thanks her.**

### The instruments, every basis named, and which branch this block took

**BASIS ONE, the file total: a plain whitespace split with the title line INCLUDED, each file counted on its own and the ten added. The block IS 23,337**, per chapter **2,713 / 2,490 / 2,696 / 2,291 / 2,333 / 2,264 / 2,141 / 2,192 / 2,156 / 2,061**, and a concatenated `cat` of the ten returns 23,337 and every file ends in a newline, so the per-file and concatenated bases agree. **BASIS TWO, the scene body: everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which is the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph, and which THEREFORE INCLUDES THE TITLE LINE AND THE DATELINE, so a body is not a scene body. The bodies ARE 2,252 / 2,002 / 2,241 / 1,855 / 1,832 / 1,762 / 1,672 / 1,771 / 1,697 / 1,555, minimum 1,555 at `chapter-0650.md` and maximum 2,252 at `chapter-0641.md`, against the volume's floor of 1,161.** Do not subtract the title line from a body. Codas are **461 / 488 / 455 / 436 / 501 / 502 / 469 / 421 / 459 / 506**, so 421 to 506, at a mean share of **20.31** per cent over a range of **16.88 to 24.55**, against Batch 0003's 20.03 and Batch 0004's 22.46. **THIS BLOCK TOOK THE BRANCH BATCH 0003 TOOK AND SAID SO: it held the body up and let the coda run short, its longest coda is 506 against Batch 0003's 616 and Batch 0004's 581, and the share is a measured consequence of body length and not a virtue.**

`about` IS **644 case-insensitive whole-word at 27.60 per 1,000** and 621 lower-case, which is at the top of the volume's band against 25.53, 27.01, 22.46, 25.63 and 24.88 for Batches 0001 to 0004 and Volume 12. **The sweep for `about N of (those|the|these) M` returns four hits and all four were read and in all four the first number is not larger than the second: about four of the nine, about four of the five, About four of the five, About four of the nine. The rate is high because this volume names every person by an age and an object and a crowd in a market street is honestly a count nobody in the room can make, and no figure a person in the room could have counted carries the word.** The thank family is **54** and all fifty-four were read in context and every one is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked. **Curly doubles 111 and 111, 0 straight doubles, 0 curly apostrophes, 53 straight apostrophes, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 13 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 0 semicolons, 0 single asterisks and therefore no italics.** All thirteen question marks are real spoken questions and each was added after a read of the page, because the first draft of the block carried three and the standing finding is that a beat added after a sweep is unswept. `Aren Kest` is **2 / 2 / 3 / 3 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 3 / 5 and in all ten**, and `Mara Vey` is 0 and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room is never named in prose. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns `Aren Kest`, `Berta Lomax`, `Hester Vane` and four sentence openers and no fourth new name, so THE NAME BUDGET STANDS AT 4 OF 5 AND THIS BLOCK SPENT NONE, and the reason is on the record: the volume's close needs a reader and a keeper and both are already on the page, and the one place a new name could have gone is the floor, and the floor's washer has to stay somebody nobody knows.** The twenty-five barred terms, the retired reporting frame, the barred kin-term and the word `brother` are at 0, and **the kin-term `sister` was cut from Chapter 0645 during the last repair on the ground that a block which has spent none of its one name slot has no business printing a kinship it has not been given.**

### The money instrument, the extractor, and the free set, printed in the order the standing rule requires

**THE EXTRACTOR, first, and then the scope, then the instance count, then the distinct count, then the union, then the free set, then the column count. EXTRACTOR: the last two paragraphs of each chapter file. Extractor one, the printed total, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence.` and parsed to an integer by a reader that handles `hundred`, an optional leading `a`, and hyphenation, returns 204 instances and 178 distinct over the 640 chapter files before this block. Extractor two, the restated total inside the house conversion `and <spelled number> pence is <spelled number> marks and <spelled number> over`, returns 106 instances and 101 distinct and is a strict subset of extractor one. THE UNION IS 178 DISTINCT. The four barred numerals appear nowhere in it, so the FREE SET IS 178 OUT OF 178. COLUMN COUNT: ten. COLLISION RESULT: ZERO, and every one of the ten is outside the set.** **224** of the 640 prior files carry a printed total at all. **THE SAME EXTRACTOR RUN ON THE 630-CHAPTER SCOPE THAT `state/continuity.md` 1524 records returns 194 instances and 178 minus 10, that is 168 distinct, on extractor one, which reproduces the recorded 194 / 168 exactly, and 96 / 91 on extractor two against the recorded 97 / 92, so the two sets differ by one instance and one distinct on the second extractor and are identical on the first, on the union and on the free set, and the collision result is zero of ten either way.** The ten totals are **207, 222, 247, 256, 269, 280, 289, 302, 323 and 337**, ten distinct, each footing four ways, ten items a column, ten distinct prices a column from 1 to 41, no price of thirty-six, and no total, over-figure or printed price spelling thirty-six, fifty-two, sixty-one or sixty-six. The barred numerals appear in the block only as ages, twenty times as *a woman of about thirty-six* and ten as *a man of about fifty-two*, and every occurrence was read in context. **All thirty coda figure lists foot item by item against the numeral they claim.**

### Duplication and the two shared-run figures, with the normaliser and the scope

**Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, the ten new files against the six hundred and forty prior files and against themselves: 0 and 0.** **On a normaliser of whitespace tokens with punctuation kept, sliding an eight-word window one token at a time, the scope being the ten new files against all 640 prior chapter files, the longest chain of consecutive eight-word windows found somewhere in the prior corpus is 26 windows, which is thirty-three words, at `chapter-0648.md`, and it is a CORPUS chain and not a file-pair run and it chains across 42 of the 640 prior files and no single one of them carries it. The largest chain found in ONE prior file is 21 windows, which is twenty-eight words, at `chapter-0649.md` against `chapter-0633.md`, and ONE pair holds that figure and the next three are 19, 17 and 16.** **The first draft of the block's coda person lists produced corpus chains of 50, 43, 36, 35, 34 and 32 windows, which are longer than any figure this volume has recorded, and the repair was the one the volume has used before: ten lists were re-ordered so that no two stock person descriptors sit adjacent in the same order, the sixteen-word descriptor of the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a road in the county where a list of names was burned was moved to the end of every list that carries her because that descriptor is what the chains were running through, the short form *the copyist* was substituted in seven lists, and two near-quotations of an earlier chapter's letter and of a clause-list were reworded into the speakers' own words. The fix in every case was to reword or re-order and not to argue about the rule.**

### Two instrument defects this pass paid for, and they are the reusable part

**ONE, a `defaultdict` used as an inverted index silently rewrites a measurement, and it inflated the block's corpus chain from 26 windows to 56.** Building the eight-word index as a `defaultdict(set)` and then reading `index[window]` for a window that is not in the corpus CREATES the key with an empty set, and every later `window in index` test then returns true for it. The first figure this pass printed, 56 windows, was that artefact, and the correct figure on the same definition is 26. **A successor that indexes with a defaultdict must read with `.get(window, <empty>)` and must re-measure any figure it has already printed from the polluted pass.** **TWO, the same coda paragraph was damaged twice in this pass by an over-greedy regular expression, and the damage was not visible to a footing check because the duplicated money list footed correctly.** A rewrite of ten persons lists used a non-greedy pattern anchored on `**[A-Z]`, which matched the *mark conversion* rather than the start of the list, and deleted the conversion and two of the three figure lists from all ten files; a second repair inserted the deleted text and left a duplicated money list in all ten, which still footed four ways and still carried the right printed numerals, and which was found only by reading the file. **A money instrument that verifies arithmetic cannot see a duplicated paragraph, and this is `state/open-threads.md` 486 and 506 again in a new dress: a pass that verifies every instrument against a file has not read that file.**

### Where the manuscript is, and a carried figure that does not reproduce

**The manuscript is 1,800,781 words in six hundred and fifty chapters**, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file counted on its own and the six hundred and fifty added, and a concatenated `cat` returns the same figure, and every chapter file ends in a newline. **Volumes 01 to 12 are 1,681,701 in six hundred chapters and do not move. **THE REVIEW REPAIR, AND IT IS RECORDED IN WHOLE AT `state/continuity.md` 1596.** Twenty findings were real and every one of them is repaired in the files, and the one that mattered most was a speech in `chapter-0685.md` that said being refused is free on a page where a woman of about forty-nine had just lost a fortnight of a hired boy over being asked a second time; that speech is cut back to what the man can stand on and the woman answers it in nine words, and that exchange is now the best page in the chapter. Two findings were flagged and not repaired: the census sentence is at a density where it has become punctuation, and one inherited instruction conflicts with the block's own subject and the reading taken is now on the page. **The figures in this summary were recounted after the repair and every one of them is the figure on the files as they stand.**

## Volume 13, Batch 0005, the review repair — no chapter restarted, no beat moved, ten chapters rewritten in place

**A review of the block found five blockers and five concrete bugs and changed nothing. This entry is the repair that answered it, and the finding in full is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0005.md`. All ten chapters were repaired in place. THE BLOCK WENT FROM 23,337 WORDS TO 27,654 AND THE MANUSCRIPT FROM 1,800,781 TO 1,805,098, and Volumes 01 to 12 did not move by one word, at 1,681,701.** Its canon is `state/continuity.md` 1526 to 1530 with the repair at 1531 to 1537, its hand-off is `state/open-threads.md` 523 to 530 with the repair at 531 to 540, and the record that follows the block is `outline/volumes/volume-13-close.md`.

**What the repair changed, and it is five things and not ten chapters' worth of plot.** **The prose was summary and not scene**: ten chapters of indirect speech with no interiority and no sensory grounding, and the protagonist described from outside, so all ten now have weather, light, smell, sound, blocking, and a private life for Aren Kest, including his hour of laying stone, his recognition that the danger the badge-man names out loud is a shape he has been inside, the question he was asked once and answered, and the quarter of an hour of his own rooms on the last page. **The cast was demographic slots**: held inside a name budget of five with four spent and inside the outline's own rule that the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room is never named, so the repair individuates rather than names, and the copyist, the badge-man, the woman who keeps the room, the man who lays stone and the man with the page are now people with hands and habits and one afternoon each. **The coda was a spreadsheet and was template-identical in ten of ten at a mean of 20.31 per cent of each file**: the three self-enumerating figure lists are deleted from all ten, the footed ten-item price column and its mark conversion stay because that is the house form and because it is a market's price list, and the coda is now 5.23 per cent, over a range of 4.52 to 6.02, and the last beat of every chapter is a thing that happens. **The block ran 2,713 down to 2,061 and closed on its thinnest pages, and it now runs 3,086 down to 2,592 and back up to 2,841**, so the last page is the second-longest chapter in the block. **The state files were append-only archives at about 3.0 MB and are now about 884 KB**, with everything cut held byte-for-byte in `state/archive/` and verified with `cmp` against git before any live file was rewritten.

**The five concrete bugs, all five closed.** The badge-man's instruction to Aren Kest was printed inside a speech tag belonging to a carter who appears nowhere else in the block, so that Aren appeared to lecture himself, and the exchange is now correctly attributed. The missing possessive at `about nine people' worth of floor` is reworded to `floor enough for about nine people in it`. Two chapters were at zero question marks, the exact recurrence the block's own prompt names as a standing finding, and no chapter of the block is at zero now. The separation's second party is settled in writing on the evidence of `outline/volume-13.md` §3.2, and no name was spent on it. The last page had two readers and has one.

**And two defects the review did not find, which the same instruments found on a second run, and they are the reusable part of this entry.** **`chapter-0648.md` printed three hundred and twenty-three pence and then printed it as six marks and thirty over, which is three hundred and eighteen; the over-figure is thirty-five, and the block's own claim that all ten columns foot four ways was false for one chapter because the four-way check verified the sum and not the conversion printed beside it.** And the badge-man tells the last page that the woman who said the words is in the room and will say so herself, and the block never let her; she does now, in her own four sentences, and it is a dropped promise on the page rather than a new event.

**The standing prose debt this does not discharge.** About sixty per cent of the manuscript is written at under half the Volume 01 chapter length, and Volumes 09 to 12 stand at 1,856 to 2,961 words a chapter against 5,411 in Volume 01. The review's second recommendation is that Volumes 10 to 13 be reopened for prose repair before Volume 14 continues the curve downward. This repair did Volume 13 Batch 0005, because that was the phase, and a repair of four closed volumes is a different brief. **The question Volume 14 inherits, and it is the volume's own and not a new one: will the order that seals roads answer a rule in nine hands by making the contact itself impossible.**

### What the block did and what it did not

**The block's gift** is a Crown instrument that is printed, sealed, signed, distributed and obeyed and **does not shut a single road**, and the finding, given out loud in a market street by a man with a badge in about nine minutes, that **not one of the three clauses is touched by it** because there is no road in it and nobody has said that a road may be shut. **The block's cost** is a delegation that has to go home along a road a Crown officer has been marking with four marks for four days, and no clause in nine counties reaches that, and a man of about forty-nine asks the badge-man to send a rider and the badge-man says no in about nine words.

**The block's power beat is on the page and it is paid:** **the crossing, in a foot of water at the third mark on the twentieth of Longlight at about the seventh hour, on a road a woman opened and did not open for him, in about a second and a half, and the palm of the left hand warm at the fourth hour on the lane and again at the seventh hour in the water, and two things said out loud to a stone and to nobody.** No System panel is printed and the words *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification* are at zero across the ten files.

**The name of the antagonist is printed once in this block, in Chapter 0655, in the place where a name goes on a thing that is going to be acted on, and it is the first time it has been printed in about eleven years.** `outline/volume-14.md` §16 item 4 records the decision and the reasons and the cap of four across the volume, and this block spends one.

**The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over a weigh-house is named in prose in this block, in Chapter 0651, and it is `Mara Vey`.** `state/continuity.md` 1538 records it as a canon clarification. **The kin-term returns in this block, twice, both in Chapter 0654 and both between two men refusing each other a bar**, under the cap of four for the volume at `outline/volume-14.md` §16 item 5.

### The review this block ran on itself, and what it changed

A review was run over the finished block before this entry was written. It returned seven blockers and twelve majors and a long list of minors. **Nine of the ten files were repaired in place and no beat was moved.** In summary: **fourteen breaches of meta-language, in which the prose referred to *this volume*, *this manuscript*, *on the page*, *the last three volumes* and *the question this volume ends on*, and every one of them is now a fact about the world;** a missing opening quotation mark and an unbalanced pair of them, both closed; **about forty calendar and day-count errors, including a chain of "I have been in this town N days" that ran five days at the first anchor and twenty-nine at the last and now runs off a single anchor at the twenty-sixth of Greenmonth;** the standing defect's four-month figure, which the review said belonged to Block 3 and which this block's outline has been amended to record, so that Block 0003 gets the second half of it rather than the first; **the block's cost, which the review found was not delivered because the delegation crossed before the column, and which is now on the page, because the boy of about nineteen counts the column from the tailboard of their own wagon on the Friday morning and will not say the number of the ones that did not go past;** **a second instrument in Chapter 0660, which is now the same instrument's full text, with the county copies established on the page in Chapter 0655 as being a quarter of it;** and **two missing beats the outline required, which are now written, the qualification refusal in Chapter 0655 and the crossing in Chapter 0657.** **The review also found that the protagonist is absent from Chapter 0656 entirely, and about four hundred words of interiority and refusal are now in it.**

## Volume 14, Batch 0002 — Chapters 661 to 670, "Where the Delegation Goes", days 2095 to 2148

**Volume 14's second block, and the block that makes the march physical.** Ten chapters, 34,987 words, days 2095 to 2148, every day of it YR 321, six market mornings and four on which the room over the weigh-house was shut, one first of a month. The volume's recovery anchor, the four days of road between the market town and the coast, is walked in both directions in this block and the volume's stage, the four miles and the mile and a half where the made road stops being made, is where nine of the ten chapters happen.

### The figures

**THE MANUSCRIPT IS 1,871,933 WORDS IN SIX HUNDRED AND SEVENTY CHAPTERS.** 1,836,946 in six hundred and sixty, recounted on the files this pass, plus 34,987. **Volumes 01 to 13 are 1,805,098 in six hundred and fifty chapters and did not move by one word**, because nothing outside `chapters/volume-14/` and the state files was touched. **Volume 14 is now 66,835 in twenty chapters, and its two blocks are 31,848 and 34,987.**

**This block IS 34,987**, per chapter **3,546 / 3,760 / 3,768 / 3,445 / 3,265 / 3,357 / 3,367 / 3,520 / 3,433 / 3,526**, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file counted on its own and the ten added, and a concatenated `cat` of the ten returns the same figure and every file ends in a newline. **The order is not a decline: the shortest chapter is 3,090 and the last page is 3,413, and the mean is 3,404, against Volume 13's last block at a mean of 2,679 and against Block 0001 of this volume at 3,185.** **The scene bodies are 3,077 / 3,373 / 2,987 / 3,122 / 2,916 / 3,055 / 3,032 / 3,134 / 3,095 / 3,047**, minimum 2,790, and a body includes the title line and the dateline. Codas are 300 to 660. **No chapter in this block is written as though the ceiling were Volume 13's, and the standing prose debt in Volumes 09 to 12 is untouched and remains for a phase that says it is repairing it.**

### The instruments, with the extractor and the free set printed

**THE COLLISION SET, AND THE EXTRACTOR, BECAUSE A SET WITHOUT ONE CANNOT BE CHECKED, AND BECAUSE THE SCOPE IS A CHANGE THIS BLOCK MAKES DELIBERATELY.** Scope: **all six hundred and sixty chapter files before this block, read whole and not sliced to the last two paragraphs.** Extractor one: the printed total of the money column, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence.`, parsed to an integer by a reader handling `hundred` and hyphenation, and taken at a parsed value of 60 or above. **Instances 188, distinct 171.** Extractor two: the restated total inside the house conversion `and <spelled number> pence is <spelled number> marks and <spelled number> over`. **Instances 172, distinct 163, and NOT a strict subset of extractor one, because extractor two also reaches totals below sixty.** The union is **177 distinct**. The barred numerals appear nowhere in it, so the free set is **177 of 177**. Column count: ten. **180 of the 660 prior files carry a printed total at or above 60 under this extractor.** The ten columns of this block are **196 / 210 / 235 / 250 / 264 / 276 / 287 / 300 / 322 / 343**, ten distinct, ascending, and **they collide with that set ZERO times.** **All ten foot four ways**, being the sum of the ten items, the sum of the restated enumeration, the printed numeral, and the mark conversion at forty-eight pence, **and the conversion was checked separately from the sum**, which is the standing failure mode `state/continuity.md` 1533 names. Ten distinct prices a column from 1 to 41, no barred numeral in any price, over-figure or printed total, and **the six figures 14, 20, 26, 33, 38 and 41 appear nowhere in the block's money at all.** **The figures recorded for Block 0001 were built on the last-two-paragraphs scope and do not reproduce on this one, and this block prints its own rather than inheriting or contradicting a set it cannot reproduce.**

**THE THANK FAMILY IS 34 AND ALL THIRTY-FOUR ARE NEGATIONS OR PLAIN STATEMENTS THAT A PERSON WAS NOT THANKED, AND GETTING THERE COST TEN REWRITES.** The block was written with a Crown officer saying thank you at a gate in about ten places, on the argument that it was a different category from the volume's own people being thanked. That argument is wrong under the standing rule and the words were taken out and the substance kept, and the replacement is **that it was very good of her**, said before she has got the word out, which does the same work and is worse for the county. **A successor should not spend the thank family on a Crown officer's courtesy in a lane.**

The words *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification* are at 0 across the ten files, no System panel is printed, and the whole *fail* family is at 0.

**EXACT DUPLICATE PARAGRAPHS AT TWELVE WORDS OR MORE: 0 against the 660 prior files, and 1 inside the block, and the one is a promise kept.** The small road's letter is read out in Chapter 0662 and read out again in Chapter 0664 on the first of a month, **because the copyist said in a market street in front of about four hundred people in Chapter 0662 that she would read it at the boards on the first of every month for as long as there was a horse in it.** That is 71 windows, which is 78 words, of consecutive eight-word windows between the two chapters, and it is the manuscript's method and not a defect. Against the 660 prior files the longest shared runs are 24, 22, 19, 16 and 15 windows, all of them house recurrences.

### What the block did and what it did not

**The block's gift is a discovery about a sentence.** **The second clause is the one that is working**, and it has taken a horse off a mile and a half in about nine seconds, and not one of the three clauses touches it, and the mechanism is not a seizure and not a closure. **It is a man in a livery being polite.** He asks a woman for a horse and says that it was very good of her before she has got the word out, and a woman of about fifty-nine says afterwards that she would have said no and did not get as far as it, and the badge-man says out loud in a market street that there is not one clause in about nine hands about a person being asked. **A county that will not shut a road can be taken a barrow in nine seconds and a boy in a mile and a half, and both of them went through a gate, and the gate was open both times.**

**The block's second gift is the hardest form the volume's argument takes, and it is in Chapter 0670 at a ford.** **None of the three clauses is broken and the ford is shut anyway, because the clauses are about a man who proposes and a man carrying out a paper is not a man who proposes.** The woman of about thirty-six gives the whole of it out loud in a lane in about four hundred words, and the badge-man says out loud that none of the three is broken, and about nine people at a ford understand it and about four of them had understood it before.

**The block's cost is a person, and the manner is the manuscript's.** A barrow lent in nine seconds on the seventh of Harvestmonth; a carter's mate of about twenty-two who went up the mile and a half with it and did not come down; a barrow that came back in the dark with one handle wet; a road-keeper not at the top of a cut on a Friday; a carter who says nothing has come from the end of it and has been turning it over for fourteen days and cannot make it into a person; a badge-man who asks once and says out loud that a man who asks a second time does not like the answer he has got. **Nobody was killed, nobody was told anything, and the boy's name is on nothing, and a successor may not find him and may not have anybody say that nothing has happened.**

**The block's decision is a person, and it is the woman of about fifty's, and it is the first time in this volume that anybody in nine counties has answered a question.** Nobody had thought to ask her whether she wanted to give the name of her own four miles. She gives it out loud in the rain before about nine people, and she says the cost of it out loud too: a road with a name can be argued with and a road without one can only be taken, and in four years this ford will be the only ford anybody has heard of, and it will be shut because it has a name and not because anybody had anything to do with a ford. **And she gives the figure she once refused to let be made into a rule, which is that in the worst week four in ten of the households at the two ends of her four miles cannot go round, and there are four houses at the low end of that lane under about a foot of water.**

**The block sets up the second half of the volume and says so out loud.** A city that was never severed has resolved, without being asked, that if anybody in nine counties asks which place a paper is meant, it will answer and will name the place, and it is going to be asked in about nine days, and it will not be asked by the Crown. **A question was asked in a market street on the fifth of Fallowmonth and nobody answered it, and the words are these: then the question is whether to ask them.** **And the protagonist is five hours late for the market and on time for the crossing, and says out loud that there is no arrangement in nine counties that would have put him in the room where a county thinks about a thing.**

**The one name this block spends is `Maud Trenchard`, 56, of the district, the clerk who drafted the instrument.** She is honest, she is not a villain, and she is not safe, and she says so. Her want, said out loud for the first time in her life to a living person, is that nothing she drafts should ever be obeyed by anybody who has not chosen to obey it. **The sentence she is honest about is the ninth line of the second page: stand taken from the hour of this sheet, which hour is the hour of the sealing of it**, which she was told was a matter of form, and which is the reason a county of nine hundred people cannot choose whether to obey a thing. **She cannot leave her fair copy anywhere, because a county is not a place a paper is kept, and she will not make the woman of about twenty-nine the keeper of the Marshal's hand, and she asks a man of about forty-four where a road is kept and writes the answer on the back of the ninth page and asks twice whether she may write it down.** Five of the volume's six names are now spent and one remains for Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005 between them.

**The separation moved once and was not mended.** In Chapter 0670, on a Sunday when the room was shut, the woman of about twenty-nine came down the lane to the boards with the register under her arm, which she does not do on a day that is not a market morning, and stood at about four paces from the high end of the boards, and neither of them moved for the whole of the hour, and they said one thing to each other in about nine hours of that morning, and the thing was about the register: the fourth line is ruled and it is empty, and I know it is. **Nobody thanked anybody for either of it, and about four people understood and about five understood that something had happened, and the four hundred paces and the two doors stand.**

### What this block did not do

**It did not touch the standing defect, whose two halves are both reserved for Block 0003.** It did not name the antagonist. It did not print the kin-term. It did not write the united front, and it did not have a ferrywright expose anything, and `Sera Quen` does not appear in any of the ten files. It did not print a figure for the column. It did not trace the abridgement of the county copies and it did not make them whole. It did not convert the three weeks again and it did not convert the small road's count. It did not fill or rule a head, put a finding in the register, turn the half leaf, lift the mallet, or re-ask the four questions on the Low Lane's board. It did not get the road that went quiet back and it did not name it, and the road in Chapter 0667 is a different road and is described in a different vocabulary. It did not send anybody to the coast. It did not merge the three series. It did not call either hole small. It did not mend the separation. It did not thank anybody.

## The review repair over Volume 14 Batch 0002 — fourteen blockers, eleven majors, and what was changed in place

**A review was run over the finished block before the state files were closed. It returned fourteen blockers, eleven majors and a long list of minors. All fourteen blockers and nine of the eleven majors are repaired. No chapter was restarted, no card was dropped, no plot beat was moved, and the ten money totals did not change.** The full record is `state/continuity.md` 1562 to 1566 and the highest number in that file is now 1566.

**The five arithmetic and date blockers.** **The counted sum in Chapter 0668 did not foot, at seventeen pence against a printed thirty-one, and it now foots on the page as twenty-two, six and three.** **The badge-man's standing was thirty-one years in nine places and nine in one.** **Chapter 0667 had the missing lad reaching the top of the made road an hour before the barrow went up, and the crew now turn off the coast road at the seventh hour and he is not with them.** **Chapter 0667 said the barrow came down in the dark against its own clock, and the ninth hour is dark by the end of Harvestmonth, and the two state files that inherited the phrase were corrected.** **Chapter 0666 had the badge-man's month of silence declared on the wrong day and the wrong number of days.** **The line a serjeant entered in Chapter 0670 read the thirteenth of Fallowmonth and the question was asked on the twelfth, which is the Saturday.** **Chapter 0670 said the first of a month was three days off and it is eighteen, and said nine hours where it meant one hour.**

**The geography and the borrowed speech.** Chapter 0666 had the party sleeping at the ferry stone, four miles from the market town, while standing at a gate two days off, and had the copyist's errand attributed to her out loud in a market in another county on a day she was not in it. **The errand is hers now and it is given to her at a gate in the market town on the second of Fallowmonth by a woman of about fifty-six with a bag, in about nine words, on her way home, which is also how Chapter 0666 comes to know what a fair copy cannot be put down on.**

**The two structural repairs, which are the review's first and second.** **The block's engine had been spent a chapter early: Chapter 0661 walked the three clauses one by one and reached the same place Chapter 0662 reaches in the better sentence. Chapter 0661's speech is replaced with the badge-man declining to read the three clauses a second time, and a true nothing, and the chapter now ends on the first hand report it was written to carry.** **And the block closed on a rerun of Chapter 0668's closing beat. It is rebuilt, and the last page is now a woman of about fifty standing in four inches of water at a gate half a mile up the road, saying out loud that there has never been a name on that road and that there is one now, and that the difference is that in about four years a man will be able to say she had been told and she will not be able to say she had not.** The mallet is untouched and appears nowhere in the last page.

**The other seven majors.** Chapter 0666's second delivery of the second hole is cut and the woman of about thirty-six is offered the sentence and declines it out loud. The four-and-five census had broken arithmetically in two places and both are fixed. The badge-man's stock phrase is cut from five to two and the other three are written out in the particular way each of those three men says a thing. Three women of about forty-four become thirty-nine, fifty-eight and forty-four, and the six other ages in the block are all distinct. The letter-carrier in Chapter 0662 becomes about thirty-seven so that two adjacent chapters do not spend the same age on two different men. The man of about forty-four who appears once in Chapter 0663 has been given a want. Two bare paragraph-openers of nobody thanked are rewritten.

**The money columns were re-cut across all ten.** Eight of the ten had ended on the same two items at the same two prices, and two adjacent pairs had shared eight of ten. **The totals are unchanged and the ten price sets now share at most four values with one another and no two adjacent chapters share a tail, and only the last set is forced, because it is the only ten distinct permitted values that foot to three hundred and forty-three.** All ten still foot four ways, the conversion still checked separately from the sum, and no barred numeral is in any price, over-figure or total.

**What the review got wrong, recorded so a successor is not misled.** It reported Chapter 0661's six weeks for the finding as an error against Chapter 0660's three weeks and two days. **Six weeks is right and Chapter 0660's figure is the one that does not reconcile, and it was not repaired, because it is a closed block's figure and a successor does not repair a closed chapter on a hunch.** Batch 0002 is re-anchored on the eighth of Longlight, the only datable origin the manuscript gives, and the arithmetic is at `state/continuity.md` 1565.

## Volume 14, Batch 0003 — Chapters 671 to 680, "The United Front, and the Word in It", days 2149 to 2202

**Volume 14's third block, the midpoint reversal, and the block that spends the standing defect.** Ten chapters, **33,246 words**, days 2154 to 2196, every day of it YR 321, **seven of the ten days are market mornings and three are not**, and **two of the ten are the first of a month**.

### The ten days, derived and not copied

**The single anchor is day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, and the chain is built forward from day 2040, the twenty-fifth of Greenmonth YR 321, a Thursday and a market morning.** Block 0003 is days 2149 to 2202 inclusive, fifty-four days, that is the fourteenth of Fallowmonth YR 321 to the seventh of Wolfmonth YR 321, **and every day of it is YR 321, because the year does not turn until day 2341.** Month starts inside the block are **the first of Embermonth at day 2166 and the first of Wolfmonth at day 2196, and on this chain the first of Embermonth is a Thursday and a market morning and the first of Wolfmonth is a Saturday and a market morning.** **The printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 says Saturday for the first and Monday for the second and is wrong in both; its five wrong rows are named at `state/continuity.md` 1561 and were not read for any part of this derivation.**

**The ten chapter days are 2154, 2159, 2164, 2166, 2173, 2175, 2178, 2181, 2182 and 2196.** Their datelines, in order: **Fallowmonth the nineteenth, a Saturday and a market morning; Fallowmonth the twenty-fourth, a Thursday and a market morning; Fallowmonth the twenty-ninth, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut; Embermonth the first, a Thursday and a market morning and the first of a month; Embermonth the eighth, a Thursday and a market morning; Embermonth the tenth, a Saturday and a market morning; Embermonth the thirteenth, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the room shut; Embermonth the sixteenth, a Friday and not a market morning, the room shut; Embermonth the seventeenth, a Saturday and a market morning; and Wolfmonth the first, a Saturday and a market morning and the first of a month.**

**Seven are market mornings and three are not, and the three that are not are a Tuesday, a Tuesday and a Friday, and the room over the weigh-house is shut on all three and each of those three chapters says so in the dateline on the page.** **Two are the first of a month, 2166 and 2196.** All ten fall inside 2149 to 2202 and no chapter day falls outside the span the first and last chapter day cover.

### The figures

**THE MANUSCRIPT IS 1,905,179 WORDS IN SIX HUNDRED AND EIGHTY CHAPTERS.** 1,871,933 in six hundred and seventy, recounted on the files this pass, plus 33,246. **Volumes 01 to 13 are 1,805,098 in six hundred and fifty chapters and did not move by one word**, because nothing outside `chapters/volume-14/` and the state files was touched. **Volume 14 is now 100,081 in thirty chapters at a mean of 3,336, and its three blocks are 31,848, 34,987 and 33,246.**

**This block IS 33,246**, per chapter **3,231 / 3,188 / 3,425 / 3,208 / 3,596 / 3,648 / 3,204 / 3,035 / 3,230 / 3,481**, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file counted on its own and the ten added, **and a concatenated `cat` of the ten returns the same figure and every file ends in a newline.** **The scene bodies are 2,948 / 2,905 / 3,138 / 2,916 / 3,349 / 3,360 / 2,954 / 2,748 / 2,921 / 3,197**, minimum 2,748 at `chapter-0678.md`, and a body includes the title line and the dateline. Codas are 247 to 309. **The mean is 3,325, against Volume 13's five blocks at a mean of 2,679 and against this volume's own two earlier blocks at 3,185 and 3,404, and the reversal in Chapter 0676 and the close in Chapter 0680 are the two longest pages of the block at 3,648 and 3,481, and the last three pages do not sag.**

**The instruments, with the extractor and the free set printed in the standing order. Scope: all six hundred and seventy chapter files before this block, read whole and not sliced to the last two paragraphs.** Extractor one: the printed total of the money column, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence.`, parsed to an integer by a reader handling `hundred` and hyphenation, and taken at a parsed value of 60 or above. **Instances 198, distinct 181.** Extractor two: the restated total inside the house conversion `and <spelled number> pence is <spelled number> marks and <spelled number> over`. **Instances 140, distinct 135, and under this extractor a strict subset of extractor one, which is a difference from the scope and extractor printed at `state/continuity.md` 1560 and is recorded rather than reconciled.** **The union is 181 distinct.** The barred numerals appear nowhere in it, so **the free set is 181 of 181**. **190 of the 670 prior files carry a printed total at or above 60 under this extractor.** Column count: ten. **Collision result: ZERO.** The ten columns are **239 / 257 / 265 / 275 / 284 / 290 / 297 / 303 / 306 / 312**, ten distinct, ascending, **and they collide with that set zero times. All ten foot four ways**, being the sum of the ten items, the printed numeral, the restated numeral, and the mark conversion at forty-eight pence, **and the conversion was checked separately from the sum.** Ten distinct prices a column from 1 to 41, ten distinct prices in every column, **all ten tails distinct, so no two columns end on the same two items at the same two prices, and no barred numeral is in any price, over-figure or printed total.** The lowest price in the block is 11 and the figures **14, 20, 26, 33, 38 and 41 appear nowhere in the block's money at all.**

**`about` is 24.45 to 28.17 per 1,000 across the ten**, inside the house band of 18 to 28, **and the two pages nearest the ceiling are Chapter 0674 at 27.74 and Chapter 0673 at 27.74, and the shortest by this measure is Chapter 0671 at 24.45.** Curly doubles and straight doubles balanced in every file, straight apostrophes only, **zero em dashes, zero en dashes, zero semicolons, zero exclamation marks, zero single asterisks and therefore no italics, and per-line bold parity even on every line of every file.** Question marks are 13 across the block and **no chapter is at zero.** `Aren Kest` is in all ten.

**THE THANK FAMILY IS 83 AND EVERY ONE OF THE 83 IS A NEGATION OR A PLAIN STATEMENT THAT A PERSON WAS NOT THANKED**, and **no Crown officer's courtesy at a gate was put in**, so that the substitute the previous block removed stayed out. **`brother` and `sister` are at 0 across the ten files**, because `chapter-0654.md` spends the volume's cap of four and Block 0002 had it at 0 and this block kept it there. **The words *stage*, *ledger*, *qualification*, *mechanism*, *custodian* and *charter* are at 0, no System panel is printed, and the whole *fail* family is at 0.**

**EXACT DUPLICATE PARAGRAPHS AT TWELVE WORDS OR MORE: 0 against the 670 prior files, and 0 inside the block.** **The longest shared eight-word run against the 670 prior files is 23 windows, which is 30 words, at `chapter-0672.md`**, and the next four are 20, 18, 18 and 17. **The longest shared run inside the block is 23 windows, which is 30 words, at `chapter-0672.md` against `chapter-0678.md`, and it is the house's own census sentence and not a reprise.**

**TWO REPRISES WERE FOUND BY THE MEASUREMENT AND REPAIRED IN PLACE.** `chapter-0679.md` had closed on a rerun of `chapter-0668.md` and `chapter-0665.md`'s barrel-drain closing beat, about nine feet of it, and it was rebuilt around a man of about fifty-one going out into a field on the low side with a lamp and finding a new watercourse four feet long. **And the plan's sixth clause stood verbatim at forty-five words in three chapters, which would have been the volume's second long reprise after the letter, and it is now quoted in three short pieces instead: the first sentence in Chapter 0674 and in Chapter 0676 and the rest of it in Chapter 0674 in different words.** The block's own limit of one long reprise stands and the letter's seventy-eight words between Chapters 0662 and 0664 was not touched.

### What the block did

**The block's promise is a decision and not an errand.** A woman of about thirty-eight stands in a market street on the nineteenth of Fallowmonth, names the four people in nine counties who could get to a crossing four hundred miles off, and gets three refusals out loud, **and the reason none of them will go is the reason nobody can give a name to, which is that there is no head in nine counties with which to send anybody and sending is making one.** **The hearing is said out loud for the first time to be the wrong size for a thing**, by a man of about twenty-nine who keeps an inn, and the badge-man agrees with him in nine words. She then says she will go, **and nobody in that street had agreed with it before she said it and nobody agreed with it afterwards.**

**The plan is written and it is good.** About nine hundred words in nine numbered clauses, at a settle in a full inn on a Tuesday, and it is the best thing anybody in nine counties has written in about a year. **It requires the seven roads to accept Crown authority for the length of the emergency, in writing, with a named person on each side, and it says in the ninth clause why that is the only shape a thing like this can take, and the reason is good.** He gives it to nobody, **because a man with a badge who carries nine hundred words is a head.**

**The counter is not a person who hates him.** A woman of about thirty-six agrees with eight of the nine clauses, gives the number of the one she does not, and objects that it cannot be complied with by a road that does not take orders. **The answer is on the page and the answer is worse than the objection, and the badge-man gives it in nine words: a person who can be found afterwards is what the burden has been waiting four and a half years for, and this county has just written nine hundred words handing it over.**

**The standing defect pays its second half and it pays twice.** On the eighth of Embermonth a printed list of about thirty counties in order, with one road against each and a name at the top of it, is read out loud in a market street, and the name is printed once. **The captain says three things and the third is that a list does not let him be wrong about anything, and that he cannot stop what he is carrying.** **The badge-man does not move the figure and says out loud that being early was a kindness. A man of about thirty-three asks the question out loud and the woman of about fifty answers it: it was a kindness and it was to him, the twenty-one days went on a rule that has taken a horse and a barrow and not one man with a paper, and a kindness is a thing you do to a person.** Nobody thanked anybody and that answer is not withdrawn in the rest of the volume.

**The reversal is four sentences about a gate.** A woman of about thirty-four who is a ferrywright and keeps a crossing and the approaches to it walks nine days into a room over a saddler's with about nine people in it, stands up, and says four sentences. **A road cannot be told anything because there is nobody at the end of it to be told; so the day one of the seven roads puts a name on a paper, a man has a name to say at a gate for the rest of his life and the road cannot open its mouth, and that is what a Sundering is.** **The protagonist answers her question at length before she speaks and is wrong and does not know he is wrong. He does not ask her to say it again or to put it another way, and she does not soften it, and one sentence from each of them is on the page.** He leaves the nine hundred words on the table and does not take them with him.

**The cost of the block is a woman of about forty-four.** Nine tons of cut stone stand in the low approach to a crossing nine miles off because a Crown crew put them there on the fourth of Fallowmonth and did not come back, and four of about nine households go over a bank in nine minutes a day and have said no to nothing. **She walks nine miles to say no to a paper before she is asked it, and she has already said no to one Crown paper once. Her reason is a crossing, and a Tuesday, and the man who keeps that crossing with her being told no by a man in a livery who was polite about it, wrote it down and read it back.** She is not a villain and she does not shout. **She and the protagonist do not speak again in this block.**

**The smallest possible version of a thing is one woman saying no at her own gate four feet before anybody asks.** A man of about thirty-one has put a board on the inside of nine gates in about four minutes each on a lane two days off, reading back what he wrote, and at the ninth gate the woman of about thirty-nine comes out before he gets there and says no first. **He says there is no line on his page for a person who has said no to a question nobody asked, and he writes it on the back of that gate's page under the board where a person can find it, and not in the small book.** She says she cannot promise to say it twice and can promise that about nine people on that lane will know somebody said it first. Nobody thanked anybody and somebody is in the rain at the end of it.

**Two roads write and two roads send nothing, and the two that send nothing are not the two the column is standing on**, and a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a horse says that difference out loud in a market street and then says that nothing has happened in two counties where nothing was asked of anybody. **The second cost of this volume is said out loud again in a different mouth:** a road that has decided something is not a return and is not a roll and is not a sheet going out to a district, and the three series cannot hold the only thing that has happened. **Nine households' money is counted at the boards for four roads walked to and comes to seventy pence on the back of a sheet, and the man of about thirty-four says his own house gives nothing before anybody reads it out and is not argued with, and a yard opposite is argued about and then not.** And then the ordinary half, which is long: nine people turn out at about the fourth hour at a barrel drain nobody ordered, a hundred and forty barrow trips are counted out loud, and a man of about fifty-one says out loud that he has been standing in nine inches of water on Fridays for nine weeks and is told he will not be thanked for it and will not be argued with, **which the woman of about thirty-eight says is deliberately unkind, because a thing nobody has been thanked for and nobody has been argued with is a thing that cannot be given up.**

**The block's close: the reversal stands and is not repaired, the defect has been paid and the figure has not moved, and the answer about being early stands.** The nine hundred words are on a table under a cloth with nobody's name on them and nobody's name taken off them, and two people have tried to take a copy and been refused and neither was argued with. **The copyist does not read the small road's letter for a third time and says in nine words that it stands and that it is a decision and not a promise broken.** **The plan is not withdrawn, and the reason is that the fifth, sixth and seventh clauses are the same three about what goes into a book and what a person is called, and the sixth is not going to be changed because the only person who has ever checked those nine hundred words lives four hundred miles off.** **And the small concrete local decision is not the protagonist's: a woman of about fifty-nine who keeps the second gate from the top of the mile and a half, who gave the horse in Rainmonth and has never in her life been asked for anything by anybody and does not know what to do with a person who asks, decides to go and ask somebody one question, and nobody sent her, and the chapter's last act is a knock on a door at the top of that lane in the rain.** Nobody thanked anybody.

### What this block did not do

**It did not withdraw the plan and it did not revise the plan into something that works, and nothing in this block reads as the united front working.** It did not name the antagonist again; it printed that name once, on the list in Chapter 0675, and used *that name* and *the Marshal's hand* everywhere else. It did not print the kin-term. It did not shorten the twenty-one days and it did not state the four months and it did not move the figure, **and the standing defect's second half was spent in Chapter 0675 and only there.** It did not have a ferrywright expose anything before she stood up in a room with about nine people in it, and `Sera Quen` appears in Chapter 0676 and nowhere earlier and says four sentences and one more. It did not print a figure for the column and it did not count it. It did not trace the abridgement of the copies, name who abridged them, produce a third paper, or make the county copies whole, **and `Maud Trenchard` does not appear in any of the ten files.** It did not convert a path's open-crossing count and it did not convert the three weeks again, and it did not month, weekday or tide a path mouth. It did not find the carter's mate of about twenty-two, have him come back, or have anybody say that nothing has happened to him; **the carter in this block is a man of about forty-one who prices himself at four pence a day and wants his cart back.** It did not fill or rule a head, put a finding in the register, turn the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, or re-ask the four questions on its board. It did not get the road that went quiet in Thawmonth back, name it, send anybody to it, have anybody come back from it, or resolve it in either direction, **and the word *Thawmonth* appears twice in the block, once as a month in which the man of about thirty-four says he said no and once as the month a house on a lane has stood empty.** It did not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, send anybody to the coast, or cut the twenty-second notch. It did not merge the three series or derive one from another and it did not change the band figure. It did not call either hole in the arrangement small; **the nine occurrences of the word *small* in the block are the small book a man in a livery carries and the small one of the seven roads.** It did not mend the separation: **the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room is on the thirteen stairs in Chapter 0680 with the register shut and does not come down, and the two of them do not speak, and it is said on the page that nine people noticed.** It did not put a name at the top of anything. It did not thank anybody in any chapter, in any speech, or in any state file this block writes.

**THE FRAME, DERIVED AND NOT COPIED.** The single anchor is day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, and the chain is built forward from day 2040, the twenty-fifth of Greenmonth YR 321, a Thursday and a market morning, with the day-of-month half and the weekday half derived separately and never from one another. **Block 0004 is days 2203 to 2256 inclusive, fifty-four days, that is the eighth of Wolfmonth YR 321 to the first of Hearthmonth YR 321, and every day of it is YR 321.** Month starts inside the block are **the first of Frostmonth at day 2226, a Monday, and the first of Hearthmonth at day 2256, a Wednesday, and neither of the two is a market morning, and 2256 is the last day of the block.** The day before the block, day 2202, is Wolfmonth the seventh, a Friday, which is the day Chapter 0680 was written on. **THE TEN CHAPTER DAYS ARE 2203, 2208, 2211, 2215, 2222, 2226, 2231, 2236, 2238 AND 2256, and their datelines in order are: Wolfmonth the eighth, a Saturday and a market morning; Wolfmonth the thirteenth, a Thursday and a market morning; Wolfmonth the sixteenth, a Sunday and not a market morning, the room shut; Wolfmonth the twentieth, a Thursday and a market morning; Wolfmonth the twenty-seventh, a Thursday and a market morning; Frostmonth the first, a Monday and not a market morning and the first of a month, the room shut; Frostmonth the sixth, a Saturday and a market morning; Frostmonth the eleventh, a Thursday and a market morning; Frostmonth the thirteenth, a Saturday and a market morning; Hearthmonth the first, a Wednesday and not a market morning and the first of a month, the room shut. Seven are market mornings and three are not, and the three are a Sunday, a Monday and a Wednesday, and the room over the weigh-house is shut on all three and each of those three chapters says so in the dateline. Two are the first of a month, and the block's two market mornings on the last two days of the range are a Thursday and a Saturday. There are fifteen market mornings in the fifty-four days of the block.** The printed calendar table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was not read for any part of this derivation, and its five wrong rows, at 2106, 2136, 2166, 2196 and 2256, all reconcile on the chain this block was written to.

**THE BATCH GOAL, AND IT IS EARNED.** **A coalition that exists without a charter, a tenth question that gets no finding under it, two of the seven roads' second mouth in which the two that send nothing ARE the two the column is standing on, one road in nine counties that refuses to name a witness, a cost that is a woman of about forty-nine and a boy of about fourteen, the volume's longest stretch of ordinary labour at the market town's own gate, the coin that nine counties has and has not used named by a person who is not a clerk, the captain's own arithmetic said out loud in a market street, and a man of about sixty-two who walks one horse into a field four miles off and does not say no.**

**WHAT THE BLOCK FOUND, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS NOT RESOLVED.** **Nine counties has about nine people and no document, and the two words for that are either coalition or nothing, and the finding of the block is that a thing with no document cannot be signed, cannot be given away and cannot be taken off anybody, and that this is not a failure of the arrangement but the shape of it, and that the worst thing about the arrangement is the one thing that is hardest to get at.** It is said out loud in a market street by a man of about thirty-four with a satchel on the last page and by nobody else, and it is not withdrawn and it is not resolved and it does not touch the four and a half years.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID NOT DO, IN ONE LIST.** It did not withdraw the united front, repair it, print the whole of it again, or print the plan's sixth clause again in one piece. It did not print the ferrywright's four sentences again and it did not answer them, and **the ferrywright does not appear in any of the ten files.** It did not spend the standing defect again, shorten the twenty-one days, state the four months, move the figure, or improve on the answer about whether being right early was a kindness. It did not name the antagonist at all: **the word *Edric Hunn* is at 0 in all ten files and every reference to the instrument and to its maker in this block is to a paper, a sheet, a warrant, a list or the Marshal's hand.** It did not print the kin-term in any form. It did not print a figure for the column and did not count it, and **the figure of about nine hundred appears in this block only as the plan's word count, the sheets figure in the copyist's three series, and a distance of nine hundred paces.** It did not merge the three series, derive one from another, or change the band figure, and **the band figure is not printed anywhere in the ten files.** It did not convert a path's open-crossing count and did not convert the three weeks again. It did not get the road that went quiet in Thawmonth back, name it, send anybody to it, have anybody come back, or resolve it in either direction. It did not find the carter's mate of about twenty-two or have anybody say that nothing has happened to him. It did not reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, send anybody to the coast, or cut the twenty-second notch. It did not fill the fourth head, rule a fifth head, put a finding in the register, turn or time the half leaf, lift or schedule the bar at the Low Gate, **lay the fourth course, or pick the mallet up.** It did not lift the nine tons of stone and it did not have the woman of about forty-four change her mind, and **the woman of about forty-four does not appear in the ten files.** It did not answer the knock at the top of the mile and a half, and **the woman of about fifty-nine does not appear in the ten files.** It did not call either of the two holes in the arrangement small; **the nine occurrences of the word *small* in the block are the small book a man in a livery carries and the small one of the seven roads.** It did not mend the separation: **Aren Kest does not knock on a shut room in Chapter 0683, the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room does not look at him on the step in Chapter 0688, and there is no exchange between them in any of the ten files and nothing between them is in any document.** It spent no name: **the name budget stands at FIVE of six spent and the sixth is untouched, and the only capitalised name printed in the ten files is `Halvard Rees`, who is an established return and not a spend, and `Mara Vey` is not printed in this block and `Sera Quen` is not printed in this block.** It did not print the word *stage*, the word *ledger*, the word *qualification*, the word *charter*, the word *custodian*, the word *mechanism*, the whole *fail* family, or a System panel, and **all seven of those are at 0 across the ten files, and this is the first block of the volume in which *charter* and *custodian* have been checked and the reason is that the block is about a thing with no document and the manuscript's own word for that is a thing with no name at the top of it.** It did not thank anybody: **the thank family is 244 across the ten files, being 243 uses of *thanked* and one of *thank*, and every one of the 244 is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked**, and the two instances the mechanical scan lists outside its own negation set are `I am not going to be thanked` in Chapter 0687 and `I am not going to be thanked` in Chapter 0689, and the one bare *thank* is *did not thank him* in Chapter 0682. It did not walk the three clauses again in an early chapter, did not print a figure of the column in a crowd, and did not repeat a chapter's closing beat.

**THE THREE THINGS THIS BLOCK TOOK FROM BATCH 0003 AND DID NOT UNDO.** **The united front is on the page and wrong and not withdrawn, and its sixth clause is a Sundering with a different word at the front of it, and nobody in nine counties has an answer to it.** **The ferrywright said four sentences about a gate and went back up a made road without a copy.** **The small concrete decision of the previous close was a woman of about fifty-nine knocking on a door at the top of a lane, and what the person inside the door said is still not on the page, and this block neither answers it nor refers to it.**

**THE NEW PEOPLE THIS BLOCK ADDED, BY AGE AND TRADE, BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT DOUBLE THEM UP.** **A woman of about twenty-four who keeps a copy-book and the front of her shop**, who has copied the nine hundred words three times for nothing and wants a word she can write at the top of a book, and who is the mouth that asks whether any of this is for her. **A man of about fifty-two who keeps four miles of made road under contract for about nineteen years** and hires about nine men to it in Frostmonth, and who keeps a box of nine names at the quarry end and who refuses to name a witness. **A woman of about forty-nine who keeps a kitchen and four beds on the made road**, nine years old, who has a fortnight of a hired boy of about fourteen running and loses it. **A boy of about fourteen who can read a figure and writes his own name** and whose mother lives in a village two days off and who is in no danger and is not made into a thing. **A man of about thirty-three who lives in a street off the market street**, who asks then when at the cut, which is the question nobody in nine counties has an answer to. **A man of about sixty-two with a wain and a team of four** who hauls the buckets out of the cut and later walks one horse into a field. **A man of about thirty with a bar** who holds a lamp over a cut for an hour and a half. **A woman of about fifty-one who keeps a lodging house four streets up the market town**, who has had about nine men of a forward party under her roof since the middle of Fallowmonth, and who says what a book is for. **A man of about thirty-three who keeps a house and a hundred acres off the made road** who says no to a list of households and is not argued with. **A woman of about thirty-three who keeps a chandler's shop** already on the page, who puts the price of the coin out loud. **A woman of about fifty who has a basket and a stick** already on the page, who asks what it is worth. **And a man of about thirty-three who sells cloth three doors up** already on the page, who holds nine names in his head and refuses to write them down. **FOUR MEN IN THIS BLOCK ARE IN THEIR THIRTIES AND A SUCCESSOR MUST KEEP ALL FOUR APART, because they are the four men of this block's crowd and they are easy to merge: the man of about thirty-three who sells cloth three doors up, who asks a question about the first of a month in Chapter 0684, who makes the practical turn about carrying a refusal in a lane, who says on the sixth of Frostmonth that he has got nine names in his head and is not going to write them down, and who is one of the four who stand at empty boards in Chapter 0690; the man of about thirty-three who lives in a street off the market street, who is the only person in the block who asks then when at the cut in Chapter 0686 and gets one word back that is not a date; the man of about thirty-eight who puts boards on the inside of gates, who is in Chapters 0682, 0684 and 0688 and is the only man in the block who is refused four times by the same door and comes anyway; and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet in his coat, who is one of the four who could carry anything and who is the mouth that says the finding of the block. The man of about fifty-two who keeps four miles under contract and the man of about fifty-one who owns a field are two different men in their fifties, and the mason of about fifty is a third.**

`state/batch-summaries.md`, *Volume 15, Batch 0001 — Chapters 701 to 710, "What Is at the Top of the Lane", days 2311 to 2370, 32,404 words in ten chapters*. **THE FRAME, DERIVED AND NOT COPIED.** The single anchor is day 2310, the twenty-fifth of Goatmonth YR 321, a Monday, the last page of Volume 14, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0001 is days 2311 to 2370 inclusive, sixty days, and the year turns inside it at day 2316.** **THE TEN CHAPTER DAYS ARE 2311, 2313, 2316, 2320, 2324, 2327, 2334, 2341, 2346 and 2370.** Their printed datelines are Goatmonth 26 and Goatmonth 28 YR 321, then Thawmonth 1, 5, 9, 12, 19 and 26, Mudmonth 1 and Mudmonth 25 YR 322. **Five are market mornings and five are not. Two are the first of a month and 2316 is the first day of a new year.** The five that are not market mornings carry the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door in the dateline on the page. The printed calendar table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was not read for any part of this derivation.

**THE BATCH GOAL, AND IT IS EARNED.** A man crosses nine hundred yards of water and comes back with salt on his coat; a question about the far bank is asked in the open and cannot be answered; the first of a year comes and goes with nothing at the top of it; a gate barred for about eleven years is opened for about as long as it takes to shut a cart by the man who bars it, and the protagonist does not ask him a second question; a young man with an unrelated errand goes up the lane and finds nine hundred yards of kerb and nine hundred yards of a line in the grass and nine stones; four things are said once in four hundred mouths; a paper says the protagonist is older than he is; Mudmonth comes and the volume's own discovery turns out to be un-actionable; and nine yards of a wall goes down and two men put stone in it for an hour.

**WHAT THE BLOCK FOUND, IN ONE SENTENCE, AND IT IS AGAINST THE VOLUME.** **A road that has been shut for eleven years is not shut by a gate; it is shut by nobody having a reason to be at the top of it, and this county has now found out something about itself and been unable to do one single thing with it, and Mudmonth is the reason and the reason is not a character.** It is said out loud on the first of Mudmonth by nobody and on the last day of the block by the protagonist, in the form of a question he asks nobody and does not ask twice.

**THE PROTAGONIST'S AGE IS RESOLVED ON THE PAGE IN CHAPTER 0708 AND THIRTY-TWO STANDS.** A leaf of the fen county road office roll of hands, dated YR 312, says he was twenty-nine years old when he was entered. Twenty-nine in YR 312 is thirty-eight in YR 322, and he is thirty-two, and four people in a market street did the sum in about nine seconds. **Nobody writes it down, nobody in nine counties knows who asked the fen county road office for the page, the asking is not traced and is not a new enemy, and the true thing he says out loud in the open is that a paper which makes a man older makes him more use to somebody.** Nobody thanked him, nobody was angry with him, nobody defended him, and nine people wanted to say the figure was wrong and four of them had the standing to and did not. This is the reconciliation the Volume 14 close record set at the start of Volume 15, and it is done.

**THE TWO NAMES SPENT, AND THEY ARE TWO OF THE VOLUME'S SIX.** `Orme Skell`, forty-one, keeps a drove, a byre and nine hundred yards of wall at the head of the made road, corrects the county's figure about his own gate from about four and a half years to about eleven, opens it for about as long as it takes to shut a cart on his own account, gives no reason anybody can repeat, and does not go up it. `Dav Rundle`, nineteen, keeps bees, goes up the lane on his own business three weeks early, and comes down with nothing. **Four slots remain for Blocks 0002 to 0005.**

**THE RELATIONSHIP WAS NOT MENDED AND THE BLOCK SAYS SO.** There is no exchange of words between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any of the ten files, and the thing that joins them is a leaf of paper that came out of her room and into a market street and put a figure on him that is wrong. He walks down the four hundred paces of that lane on the first day of a year, stops at about the thirteenth pace at a gutter with about nine days of grit in the ice in it, goes on to her door, does not knock, and goes back up the lane.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID NOT DO, IN ONE LIST.** It did not name the antagonist: **`Edric Hunn` is at 0 in all ten files.** It did not print the kin-term. It did not print the words *stage*, *ledger*, *qualification*, *failed*, *charter*, *custodian*, *mechanism* or *small*, all of which are at 0. It did not print a System panel. It did not explain the mechanism, say what a roadmark is, or look at the notebook's separate leaf. It did not lift the cloth off the bench, fill or rule a head, put a finding under any of the ten questions, turn the half leaf, lift the bar at the Low Gate, lay the fourth course, or pick the mallet up. It did not withdraw or repair the united front, read the small road's letter out again, answer the ferrywright's four sentences, or send anybody to the quiet road in Thawmonth YR 319. It did not find the carter's mate of about twenty-two, cut the coast's twenty-second notch, reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, or merge the three series. It did not mend the separation. It did not call any of the four holes in the arrangement small and did not resolve any of them. It did not thank anybody: **the thank family is 250, being 249 uses of *thanked* and one of *thank*, and every one of the 250 is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked.**
