THE TEN CHAPTER DAYS ARE 2371, 2374, 2406, 2412, 2413, 2416, 2418, 2422, 2424 and 2427**, printed as Mudmonth 26 and Mudmonth 29 and Longlight 1, 7, 8, 11, 13, 17, 19 and 22. **Five are market mornings and five are not**, at 2371, 2406, 2413, 2418 and 2427. **One is the first of a month, Longlight the first, and it is a Saturday market morning, which is why this is the first first of a month in about four and a half years to arrive with four hundred people standing about nine feet off it.** **The block's last chapter is day 2427 and its last day is 2430, so the last three days carry no chapter of their own and fall inside Chapter 0720's span.** The printed calendar table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was not read for any day of this block. **MUDMONTH YR 322 IS SIXTY DAYS LONG AND THIS BLOCK IS WHERE IT IS FIXED**, because Mudmonth the first is day 2346 and Longlight the first is day 2406, and 2406 is derived twice and independently from Volume 14's own chain and from a year of twelve carried thirty-day months. **No chapter of this block prints a day of month above thirty, because no chapter is dated in days 2376 to 2405.**

**THE BATCH GOAL, AND IT IS EARNED.** The shoulder dries in Longlight; five people say out loud in a market street, each in their own trade, why they are going up a lane on their own business; the badge-man says what a party is and it has no head and no paper; five people and a sixth are on that shoulder for eleven days; two of nine households walk the line of nine stones with one outsider and no further and say why two and not nine in their own trade; three strokes of a thing being struck are heard once at about the ninth hour and are not a fact anybody can build on; a crossing that is not there is said out loud by a man with no standing to say it and is not argued with and is not thanked; the party comes down the lane and goes five ways and gives no account of anything to anybody; and two trades in one market street disagree in the open about whether four hundred people who have heard something are in a different position from four hundred people who have not.

**WHAT THE BLOCK FOUND, IN ONE SENTENCE.** **The whole of what this county has about the oldest road it knows of is a rota of beasts that has been kept about nine years without a head on it, thirty-one words about a winter, twenty-three words about what is on the far side of nine stones, three strokes heard once, and a crossing that is not there — and a party of five people who each paid for it in their own trade and went home without giving an account of anything to anybody.** It is the volume's second cost in kind: not a fact four people know, but a thing two households will not come four feet further, in a rota nobody has a paper for.

**THE GIFT.** The shoulder came up on the nineteenth of Mudmonth and the lane at its foot came out of the water on the twenty-eighth, and about nine days passed in which the ground was firm and there was no way of getting to it. On the first of Longlight the five reasons are given out loud in a market street and the badge-man prices the thing itself in fifty-six words: **there are five people going up a lane in this county on their own business and there is no head of it and there is no paper of it, and sending one of them would make a head, and nobody in this street has sent anybody.** Longlight is the month a boundary oath-stone is renewed in this world and about nine people in nine counties know it and the cloth seller says in twenty-four words that there is no oath-stone in this county and no boundary on that made road to renew and that a month is a month, and nobody argues with him and nobody agrees with him.

**THE TEST.** Two of the nine households came up to the line of the stones on the ninth of Longlight and one outsider came with them and the other five stayed nine hundred yards back. **Seven did not come once and were asked nothing, and the reason is not cowardice and is not courtesy and is not fear of strangers: it is a rota of beasts, kept about nine years, not broken once in the nine years, and seven of the nine had stock on the flat in the week of the eleventh of Longlight and two did not.** The man who keeps the far end of that lane says so out loud in his own trade in thirty-nine words and finishes with **anybody who says it is fear or manners is not keeping beasts**. The two say it for themselves in forty-five words and thirty-one words, and the first of them says out loud in thirty words why the outsider is wanted at a line of stones: **you are not one of us and you have not got a beast on that flat and that is the whole of what I want at a line of stones.**

**THE COST.** The man of thirty-two says out loud on a shoulder, once, in fifty-four words, that there is no crossing at these nine stones and that the whole of what this party came up here to do cannot be done from this side, and that he has got no standing in this county to say so, and that he is saying it. **Nobody argued with him and nobody thanked him.** The five then count their own eleven days out in five trades and four figures come out of it, and the fifth man refuses in the open to give his own cost and nobody asks him.

**THE ORDINARY HALF, AND IT IS LONG.** Forty-eight days from a frost to a market morning and one frame of brood where there were nine; ninety-nine days and three frames where there were nine; thirty-four days of water in a lane and twenty-eight days of nine yards of a wall down by the twenty-sixth of Mudmonth and thirty-one by the twenty-ninth; a drove on Thursdays through a gap in a wall about nine feet wide that has never been a gate; the hives in a walled yard half a mile below the wall; the market street on Saturdays with about four hundred people and about two hundred of them stopped and the same proportion since the first of Embermonth; the weigh-house and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month; a causeway about nine hundred paces long crossed on foot with the water four hundred yards wide; and about four hundred people who have heard four things said out loud and have not acted on any of them.

**THE CLOSE, AND IT IS NOT THE SHOULDER AND NOT THE STONES.** On the twenty-second of Longlight, a Saturday market morning, the man of about fifty-eight with a badge and no head says the true thing about what a hearing is worth in forty-seven words — **a hearing is worth the paper it makes and this one has made nothing in four and a half years, and what it made is worth what the Marshal's hand makes of it, which is nothing** — and the man of about thirty-three who sells cloth three doors up answers in his own trade in sixty-six words that four hundred who have heard are not in the same place as four hundred who have not, because a person who has heard a thing can be found and a person who has not cannot. **They disagree in the open and nobody agrees with either of them and nobody takes a side and nobody thanks anybody.** **THE QUESTION THE READER IS LEFT WITH IS THE ONE THE PROTAGONIST CANNOT ASK, AND IT IS WHETHER FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE WHO HAVE HEARD THE SAME FOUR THINGS ARE IN A DIFFERENT POSITION FROM FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE WHO HAVE NOT. He worked it out at about the tenth hour at the sea end of that street and did not say it out loud, because asking it would be sending, and a man who asks a market street whether four hundred people are a thing has made four hundred people into a thing.**

**THE ONE NAME, AND IT IS SPENT ONCE.** `Tarr Lyster`, about fifty-four, keeps the far end of that lane, which is to say he keeps the boundary of the common the nine households share, and he looks at it about four times a year and about nine years, and he is named once in Chapter 0716 in the narration the way `Dav Rundle` was named, and the other two references to him in that chapter are not by name. **The volume's six-name budget stands at THREE of six with three slots unspent for Blocks 0003 to 0005.** `Iven` and `Marrow` were both rejected because both are established names in earlier volumes.

**THE RELATIONSHIP WAS NOT MENDED AND THE BLOCK PUTS ONE NEW BEAT ON THE PAGE.** No exchange of words between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any of the ten files. **In Chapter 0711, on a market morning, he goes up the thirteen stairs for the first time anybody in nine counties has seen him in that room in about four and a half years, and there are two people in the room, and neither of them says anything to him and he says nothing to either of them, and he looks at a press standing open with no date on it and comes down.** The copyist of about twenty-six is in the room and the woman who keeps it is in the room. The register gains no entry, the day-book stands at eleven entries, the fourth ruled head is empty and no fifth is ruled. Nothing between them is in any document.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID NOT DO, IN ONE LIST.** It did not have anybody cross the nine stones and it did not show what is on the other side of them. It did not have nine households walk the stones. It did not have a cart appear a second time as a fact. It did not name the antagonist: **`Edric Hunn` is at 0 in all ten files and the volume's cap of two is untouched, and the single reference in the block is *the Marshal's hand*.** It did not print the kin-term. It did not print a System panel. It did not print the words *stage*, *ledger*, *qualification*, *failed*, *charter*, *custodian*, *mechanism* or *small*, all at 0. It did not explain the mechanism, say what a roadmark is, or look at the notebook's separate leaf. It did not lift the cloth off the bench. It did not fill or rule a head, put a finding under any of the ten questions, turn the half leaf, lift the bar at the Low Gate, lay the fourth course, or pick the mallet up, and the sill and the mallet are not in the block at all. It did not turn the four written refusals of Volume 14 into a body, a practice, a rule or a head. It did not mend the separation. It did not ask the protagonist a second question about the gate, have the man of about forty-one explain himself twice, or resolve whether the first question went unasked on purpose. It did not re-open the protagonist's age. It did not trace who asked for the roll. It did not call any of the four holes small or resolve any of them. It did not get the quiet road back, find the carter's mate, cut the notch, reopen the shut road four hundred miles off, or send anybody to the city. It did not merge the three series or print the band figure. It did not convert a path count into days. It did not have the column come back up the made road. It did not thank anybody: **the thank family is 213, being 211 uses of *thanked* and two of the bare *thank* over the ten files of Chapters 711 to 720, and every one of the 213 is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked. A later review proposed that this figure was unreproducible and was wrong; it had counted Chapters 0710 to 0719.**

## Volume 15, Batch 0003 — Chapters 721 to 730, "The Road That Stops Being Made", days 2431 to 2490

**The ten days are 2432, 2434, 2435, 2436, 2439, 2443, 2448, 2452, 2466 and 2490, printed as Longlight 27, Longlight 29, Longlight 30, Rainmonth 1, Rainmonth 4, Rainmonth 8, Rainmonth 13, Rainmonth 17, Harvestmonth 1 and Harvestmonth 25, all YR 322. Five are market mornings and five are not, and two are the first of a month. The block crosses two month boundaries and not one, at day 2436 and at day 2466, and carries no day of month above thirty.**

**THE BLOCK IS 34,707 WORDS IN TEN CHAPTERS**, per chapter **3,329 / 3,423 / 3,469 / 3,424 / 3,438 / 3,836 / 3,364 / 3,498 / 3,401 / 3,524**, mean **3,470**, against Batch 0002's post-repair mean of 3,400 and Volume 14's volume mean of 3,449. `about` runs **24.99 to 26.59 per 1,000** with no page at or above 28.00, counted case-sensitively over the file total. Every file ends in a newline. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns nothing at all and the block spends no name, so the volume's six-name budget stands at three of six with three slots unspent.** The ten money totals are 66, 100, 113, 126, 140, 155, 163, 169, 310 and 328 pence, ten distinct, ascending, ten items and ten distinct prices a column, no barred numeral in any price, over-figure or total, ten distinct tails, every column footing four ways and the marks conversion checked separately from the sum, and **zero collisions against seven hundred and twenty prior files read whole.** **The block holds ZERO exact duplicate paragraphs of twelve words or more, and the two that stood here on the first pass have been repaired, one in each of two chapters, and neither repair changed the plot.**

**WHAT HAPPENED.** The broom on that east rise came on about the fourth of Rainmonth, and a young man of about nineteen said it out loud in a market street before he went and then went up two miles of unmade road at the sixth hour behind the Thursday drove, which is the only errand in this county that already goes up that lane, and the drove came back down at about the fifth hour and he did not go with it, and a man of about thirty-two with a stick went up with it and did not go with it either. **Nobody thanked him and nobody sent anybody, and it is the first time in about four and a half years that anybody in nine counties has gone up that lane for the thing the lane is actually for, and the thing is a broom.** Four bundles came down on the eighth, about nine brooms and about nine bee brushes were made out of them, nine people took one off a board for nothing and about five did not, and there is now a broom standing in the house of the man of about forty-one and he did not buy it and nobody has said that he did and nobody has said that he did not.

**THE FINDING, IN HIS OWN ORDER.** Nine hundred yards of kerbed road, then nine hundred yards of a line nine feet wide in the grass, then nine stones at the end of it, and the young man walked it twice and it is where he said it was. The line is nine feet wide because the east rise is cut along it once a year, and a line in grass that is not cut closes over in about three years, and he has seen two other nine-foot lines in this county and one was gone in two years and one was gone in five. Nine households past those nine stones keep a rota of that flat, cut the grass up to the stones and do not go past them, and have not broken it once in the nine years, and nobody else cuts any of it, and the kerb is older than the cutting and nobody in nine counties can say by how much. When the man of thirty-two asked out loud, on that shoulder, what it is for, the answer was that it is not for anything, it is a boundary first and it is nine hundred yards of it. **And the second half of the finding is twelve words: a thing that is found is not a thing that is used.** **Nobody crossed the nine stones and nothing on the other side of them was seen by anybody on this side of them.**

**THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL IS ONE PARAGRAPH AND NOBODY NOTICES IT.** On the fourth day on that shoulder, at about the fourth hour, the left palm of the man of thirty-two was warm and there was a thing at it that would have taken him to about the ninth hour of a night he cannot put a year to, and he stood with his hand open and looked at the ground at his own feet for about as long as it takes to turn a cart. It was a transfer and he witnessed it and did not conduct it. He told nobody, nobody asked him, nobody noticed the palm, and about as long as it takes to turn a cart later he cut nine inches off a stalk of broom and put it inside his coat and nobody asked him what he was doing. No System panel, no route opened or closed or cut, the Many-Handed not crossed, the separate leaf not looked at, the mechanism not explained.

**THE TWO ACCOUNTS OF THAT ROAD WERE PUT IN FRONT OF EACH OTHER AND THEY DO NOT AGREE.** On Longlight the twenty-seventh a cloth seller said out loud, in his own trade, that a road that goes between two places has both of the places under the grass, and an hour later and not in answer a roadkeeper put his hand flat on nine feet of a new kerb and said out loud that a road that goes across a shoulder and does not go off it goes to a place that is not there any more, and the cloth seller said then it is a lane, and the roadkeeper said one word, which was no, and **neither of them said where the kerb stops.** On Harvestmonth the twenty-fifth the same two trades priced about four and a half years out loud and got two answers and nobody agreed with either. **And about nine hundred yards of an old road is now in about four hundred mouths and in nobody's hand, and a man of thirty-two worked out at the sea end of that market street whether nine households that have cut that grass for nine years without knowing it was a road are a thing anybody can be asked a question about, and did not ask it, because asking it would be making a claim and a man with no standing who makes a claim about nine people who have never been asked anything has made nine people into a body, and those nine households are not a body and keep a rota about beasts and not a head and not a paper.**

**THE ORDINARY HALF, IN FIGURES.** About seven of the nine yards of wall are back in and the gap is about two yards and about four feet high at the close of the block, and the man of about forty-one has carried stone about two miles on his own since the second of Mudmonth, which is a hundred and forty-three days at the close of the block. The bar at the head of the made road is in on the lane side and has not been asked about. The drove has gone up that lane about nine hundred yards on five Thursdays since the second of Mudmonth and stood in the field and came back at about the fifth hour, and nobody went with it. The hives stand at three frames of brood where there were nine, and it is a hundred and sixty-seven days since the night of the eighth of Thawmonth. The hearing has not been called since the first of Goatmonth, cannot be called and does not sit, and its firsts came and gone seven times running with nothing at the boards.

## Volume 15, Batch 0004 — Chapters 731 to 740, "Asked, and Refused Twice", days 2491 to 2550

**THE BLOCK IS THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND IT IS TWO REFUSALS.** On the fourteenth of Fallowmonth a man of thirty-two with a stick asks a man of about fifty-four who keeps the far end of a shut lane, out loud and in the open on a shoulder two miles above a made road, for one thing: to go past the ninth of the nine stones and stand on the flat beyond them and come back down and say what is on it. The question is twenty-six words. The refusal is eighty-five words and gives its reason inside the same breath, and the reason is that he cannot answer for nine people and is not going to be asked to be a head. The acceptance is thirty-one words and takes about as long as it takes to turn a cart. Twelve days later the same one thing is asked out loud and in the open in a market street with nine people in it, of a clerk of that county's road office, and the answer is thirty-four words in four sentences and explains no part of itself: **No. That is not a thing this office has an entry for. I will write down that a man said it out loud in a market street. Nothing will be done with the paper.** The second acceptance is twenty-eight words. Neither man had refused anybody before, neither was under any obligation, neither was refusing him, and nobody thanked either of them.

**THE SENTENCE THAT ENDS IT, AND IT IS FORTY-FIVE WORDS AND NOBODY ARGUED WITH IT.** On the twenty-eighth of Fallowmonth, a Thursday and a market morning, with about four hundred people in that street: **I have asked two people in two places in twelve days for the same one thing and both of them have said no and I am not going to ask either of them again and I am not going to argue with either of them.** Two days later a badge-man who has not asked anybody anything in about four and a half years asks one question about a decision and not about a road, and the answer is no in fifty-three words, said with the left hand hanging where anybody could see it: **No. I am not going to hold it and I am not going to give it to anybody, and there is a thing at my left hand and I am not going to use it, and I am not going to say what it is and you are not going to ask me.** **THE THING IS STILL THERE AND IS STILL WARM ABOUT FOUR DAYS IN SEVEN AND HE HAS NOT SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NEVER GOING TO USE IT, and what the no cost him is that one other person in nine counties now knows there is a thing at that palm, and he chose not to conceal it and not to explain it, and that is a new act and not the one on the shoulder.** Nobody noticed the warmth. Nobody consoled him. Nobody thanked him. He does not get a page of grief and the narration gets it in four sentences.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DECIDED ON THE PAGE ABOUT THE FAR SIDE OF THE NINE STONES.** It does not show it, and it says why in Chapter 0737 and again in the narration of that chapter: a refused man who looks over a boundary from where he was refused has turned a refusal into a sight, and a refusal which can be turned into a sight was never a refusal. Nobody has been past the ninth of the nine stones since the seventeenth of Longlight and nobody crosses them in this block.

**THE ORDINARY HALF, IN FIGURES, AND FOUR OF THEM MOVED INSIDE THE BLOCK.** Eight of the nine yards of wall are back in throughout and the gap is about a yard and about four feet high and the last yard has not been started, and one man has carried stone two miles in his own hands since the second of Mudmonth, which is a hundred and forty-eight days on the first day of this block and two hundred and three on the last. The drove has gone up that lane on every Thursday since the second of Mudmonth and that is twenty-two drove on the thirtieth of Harvestmonth and twenty-nine on the last Thursday of the block. **The brood has gone from three frames where there were nine on the night of the eighth of Thawmonth to five, and the fourth frame was drawn on the second of Fallowmonth and was full by the sixth of Embermonth and the fifth frame came in on the twentieth, and this is a thing that happens in a trade and is not a finding.** About four hundred and nine parish rolls come past that floor in a good month and about two hundred and forty have come past it in twenty-five days of Embermonth. The hearing's firsts came and gone ten times running with nothing at the boards. One sheet of paper is in an office four days off that cannot do anything with a sheet. The bar of wood, the fourth course of stone, the mallet, the three courses of sill, the half leaf and the bench under the cloth did not move.

**THE MONEY, AND THE ROUTE THIS BLOCK TOOK, WHICH IS THE ONE 1649 CALLS CLEANER.** The extractor is the printed total, taken as the spelled phrase in every spelling this corpus prints plus the digit form, over all seven hundred and thirty prior files read whole, and it leaves SEVEN values in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three that no prior file has printed, being 136, 166, 232, 236, 266, 332 and 336, of which 266 carries a barred over-figure, leaving six usable. **THE BLOCK'S TEN TOTALS ARE 55, 56, 57, 58, 59, 136, 166, 232, 236 AND 332, five of them below the extractor's anchor of sixty and five of them from the six usable free values, so that not one column in this block collides with anything printed in seven hundred and thirty prior chapters.** The over-figures are 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 40, 22, 40, 44 and 44, the barred six are absent from every price, over-figure and total, and the highest total is 332 inside the true ceiling of 343 and not inside the 332 that older records print as the ceiling.

One question mark in every file. Zero em dashes, en dashes, semicolons, exclamation marks, single asterisks, straight double quotes and curly apostrophes. The thank family is 156 and every one of the 156 is a negation. `Edric Hunn` is at 0 and both of the volume's two printings stand available to Batch 0005. **THE BLOCK CONTIBUTES ZERO EXACT DUPLICATE PARAGRAPHS ON ITS OWN INSTRUMENT, AND THAT STILL STANDS AFTER THE REPAIR.**

## Volume 15, Batch 0005 — Chapters 741 to 750, "The Mapped Scar", days 2551 to 2610

**THE FIFTH AND LAST BLOCK OF VOLUME 15, AND THE BLOCK IN WHICH THE MANY-HANDED IS CROSSED AND THE VOLUME'S ONE SYSTEM PANEL IS SPENT.** Ten chapters, 32,943 words, days 2551 to 2610, one month boundary at day 2556, five market mornings and five that are not, and the last chapter on the volume's last day.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID.** A light came on the top of that shoulder at about the fourth hour of the morning of the twenty-sixth of Embermonth and one man saw it and told nobody, and it came back on every morning after that, and on the sixth morning four people saw all nine hundred yards of it at the same time. A roadkeeper with four miles of made road under contract for about nineteen years drew nine hundred yards of it in charcoal on the back of his board, put the board up on the low step of a weigh-house in a market street with about four hundred people in it, about nine of whom came and looked, and then took it down out of the rain and has not put it up since. That board is the only map of anything anybody in nine counties has ever drawn and **about nine people in nine counties can now point at a thing on it**, and the thing is a scar nine feet wide and about nine hundred yards long, dead in the grass, not burnt, not cured, not going to close over, and nobody says it is small.

**THE ASKING, WHICH IS THE COST.** On the first of Wolfmonth he asked four people out loud, in the open, in their own trades, for one thing each. Three said yes in their own trades and not as a favour. The man of forty-one who keeps the drove and the byre and about nine hundred yards of wall did not answer, and the chapter says plainly that that is not a refusal.

**THE CROSSING, IN CHAPTER 0746.** On the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth at about the fourth hour he held ten routes against the light while three other people stood on the grass, and the volume's one System panel is spent there and nowhere else in the volume, alone as its own paragraph, carrying no number: **What is held by one hand is taken by one hand.** The cost is on the page and is not cured: he cannot get off that grass on the first attempt, and he cannot hear from the side his stick is on, and on the seventh of Frostmonth he says out loud that he cannot go up that hill on his own.

**THE ORIGIN, ANSWERED, AND IT IS A BOUNDARY AND A REFUSAL.** On the seventh of Frostmonth, on the grass at the ninth of the nine stones, in 122 words printed in full: the scar is in the boundary and not in the kerbed road; the nine hundred yards of kerb is nine hundred yards of road with not one yard of the scar in it; nine stones at one height across the top of nine hundred yards are a thing a man on the made road at the bottom could read, and they put them there and they never walked up it, and there is nobody to find. **No body, no reunion, no notes, and nobody comes down any road with one.**

**THE RESOLUTION, AND IT IS NEGATIVE.** Both refusals of Block 0004 stand and the acceptance stands and neither is argued with. **The question the volume ends on is who is going to hold nine hundred yards of wall.** The badge-man asks it out loud at the boards on the fifteenth of Frostmonth in seventy-three words and nobody answers it, and on the twenty-fifth a roadkeeper and a beekeeper give two answers that do not agree and a beekeeper and a badge-man and a man of thirty-two all say nothing. **It is worse than the question the volume opened with, which was where the oldest road goes, and nobody in nine counties is going to be told that either.**

**THE THREE THINGS THE VOLUME HANDS ON, IN ITS OWN TERMS.** A leaf with one thumb on it, read out at the boards in seventeen words, saying that a place is shutting its own end before Hearthmonth and is not asking anybody for anything. A wall that is on the back of a board instead of hidden behind about nine hundred yards of nine-foot grass. And a piece of paper from the road office four hundred miles off asking this county for the name of a person and a copy of a drawing, which this county cannot answer and has been unable to answer since the first of Goatmonth YR 321.

**THE RELATIONSHIP WORK.** Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over the weigh-house are further apart at the close of this volume than at the close of Volume 14, and each of them has built one thing that does not depend on the other. There is no exchange of words between them in any of the ten files and nothing between them in any document. Hers is a second lamp at the foot of the thirteen stairs, lit at the fourth hour, paid for out of fourpence a year, and there was no lamp on those stairs before. His is two mile stones between the seventh mile stone and the ninth put back in line about the middle of Harvestmonth with a bar and a lever and no mortar.

**THE NAME BUDGET CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX SPENT AND TWO UNSPENDED, AND THIS BLOCK SPENT NONE.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns nothing that is not an established name, a place, or an artefact of the money coda.

**THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IS SPENT TWICE IN THE VOLUME AND BOTH ARE DOCUMENTS,** at `chapter-0745.md` and `chapter-0749.md`, both inside a printed sheet read out loud at the boards, and both inside the volume's cap of two.

**THE MONEY, WITH THE FINDING NOBODY HAD RUN.** The extractor is the printed total, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence`, taken in every spelling this corpus prints, being `one hundred and X pence` and `a hundred and X pence`, plus the digit form `N pence`, over all seven hundred and forty prior files read whole. It leaves twenty free values in the band sixty to three hundred and forty-three, and **twelve of those twenty carry an over-figure that is one of the barred six, because the over-figure of a column is its total modulo forty-eight. The five-block series on Basis B is 83.4, 91.1, 89.6, 80.6 and 56.4, and on Basis A it is 74.9, 82.0, 78.2, 71.0 and 46.8. **The seventy-five per cent ceiling set at `state/open-threads.md` 647 is crossed in this block for the first time in the volume, on both bases, and it was crossed by writing the ordinary half of a day as work, weather, distance and silence and not by cutting the census out of the beats that need it.**

THE THIRTY-THREE AND THIRTY-FOUR THIS ENTRY FIRST PRINTED ARE WITHDRAWN AND WERE WRONG BY TWO ON BOTH FIGURES; the instrument is a paragraph split on the blank line, internal whitespace normalised, twelve words or more, with `.get(key, 0)` and never `key[key]`.** Nine verbatim repeats that the block's own first pass produced, against `chapter-0731.md`, `chapter-0732.md`, `chapter-0739.md` and `chapter-0740.md`, were re-cut on the page before the block closed. **Two verbatim sentence runs inside the block, being the inventory line *Two refusals are in about four hundred mouths and in nobody's hand either* at 0741 and 0750 and the inn-and-the-cloth frame at five files, are the volume's named recurrences and sit below the paragraph-level instrument and are not defects. `state/continuity.md` 1696.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0001 — Chapters 751 to 760, "One Thing Each", days 2611 to 2682

**This is the FIRST BLOCK OF VOLUME 16 AND IT IS THE SEVENTEENTH BLOCK THIS PROJECT HAS WRITTEN SINCE THE MANUSCRIPT OPENED, and the volume's own instruction to a writer is `outline/volume-16.md`, which this phase also wrote, and no Volume 16 block prompt existed before this one.** The block is ten chapters on days 2611 to 2682, seventy-two days, crossing three month boundaries at 2616, 2646 and 2676, and the last boundary is the first day of YR 323 and Chapter 0760 is the first page in the manuscript dated in YR 323. Five datelines are market mornings and five are not, and the five that are not carry the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door on the page.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID.** It began a practice. At about the fourth hour before light, on the five mornings a week the room over the weigh-house is shut, a number is said out loud on the weigh-house floor four days off by one person and said again by a second, and the number is how many of ten things are being held at that hour by somebody else. The rule is fifty-two words at `chapter-0751.md` and it is that two people must say out loud in the same hour that they are holding a thing and both must still be holding it on the next morning they said they would be. Nine people were asked in nine trades over seventy-two days and six of them said yes. The number on the ten datelines is 3, 3, 4, 4, 5, 5, 6, 6, 8 and 8, and the practice has run 1, 4, 10, 14, 19, 24, 28, 34, 39 and 52 mornings. **The practice is not written down anywhere in the block, on purpose, and the reason is said out loud in fifty-three words, and it is the first failure and not an oversight.**

**WHAT IT COST.** A lime cart that has come up a cart track and eleven miles of made road from the north on every second Thursday for about nine years and about two hundred and thirty times did not come on the twenty-seventh of Hearthmonth, being day 2642, and nothing has come since. The figure that found it was the man nobody had ever asked anything: `Renn Ottery`, about fifty-four, who said the number of loads out loud in fifty-three words and stated that he has never once been one out in about four and a half years. Nine hurdles were found across the cart track at the head of it, foot to foot, with a hurdle shoe on the top one and a thumb of lime on it, and nine houses about four hundred yards back with not one light in any of them, by the roadkeeper of about fifty-two going up on his own business because the kerb he is under contract for is on the first nine miles of that made road. **Two of the ten things are not held at the close of the block: the Wethergill end, which went quiet on the thirteenth of Hearthmonth and which nobody has been up for thirty-six days, and the scar, which has never been held by anybody at all.**

**WHAT ARRIVED.** A printed notice in the ordinary post from the office four hundred miles off, read out loud at the boards in two sentences and seventy-five words, under the Marshal's hand and with no name at the foot of it: by the first of Mudmonth every road in nine counties is to be entered in a roll with the name of the person who keeps it set against it, any road with no name against it is to be shut, and the county is not to keep a copy of the roll. **This county has now been asked for one name twice in two forms in ninety days, by the same office, in the same building, and has answered neither time, and it has four roads with no name against them, and the man who could put one of the four names down said out loud in thirty-four words on a shoulder that he will hold the wall and that the wall has got no name against it.**

**THE RELATIONSHIP.** The first exchange of words between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in about four and a half years is at `chapter-0758.md`, at about the fifth hour, on a lane, at the bottom of thirteen stairs, and it is about a lamp. He says it in forty-one words and she says it back in thirty-eight, and neither of them said what the lamp was for, and nothing about it is in any document, the register gains no entry and the day-book stands at eleven entries. **Block 0001 does not mend the separation and does not choose a household, and the series row's shared household is a Block 0003 event.**

**THE TWO NAMES, AND THE BUDGET.** `Renn Ottery` and `Pell Arkwright`, the second of them named out of a cart tally read aloud in that market street, which is the only document in this county that has ever carried the name of a man who drives a cart. The Volume 16 budget is six and this block spent two, and neither is one of Volume 15's two unspent slots, which stay unspent. `Aren Kest` is printed once in the block and it is the volume's first page, and the capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns those three strings and nothing else.

**THE MONEY, AND THE FINDING NOBODY HAD RUN.** The ten totals are 168, 171, 175, 180, 187, 194, 202, 211, 221 and 232 pence, ten distinct and ascending, and every column foots four ways with the forty-eight-pence conversion checked separately from the sum, and the over-figures are 24, 27, 31, 36, 43, 2, 10, 19, 29 and 40, and none of the barred six of 14, 20, 26, 33, 38 and 41 is a price, an over-figure or a printed total. **THE INSTRUMENT PRINTED AT `state/continuity.md` 1690 IS INCOMPLETE AND THIS BLOCK FOUND IT: it matches `one hundred and X pence` and `a hundred and X pence` and the digit form, which covers one hundred to one hundred and ninety-nine and does not cover the two-hundred and three-hundred band this manuscript prints. RUN COMPLETE OVER ALL SEVEN HUNDRED AND FIFTY FILES READ WINSTRUMENT, GIVEN WHOLE OVER ALL SEVEN HUNDRED AND FIFTY PRIOR FILES, IT GIVES TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY-FOUR FREE VALUES IN THE BAND SIXTY TO THREE HUNDRED AND FORTY-THREE, OF WHICH TWO HUNDRED AND FOURTEEN SURVIVE THE BARRED-OVER-FIGURE FILTER.** **AND THE ROUTE BELOW SIXTY IS NOW SHUT, because ten distinct non-barred prices cannot sum below fifty-five and every total from fifty-five to fifty-nine is printed and the only free value under sixty is fifty-two, which no ten distinct prices can reach, and this block therefore takes all ten of its totals from the band and says so on the page in all ten codas.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0002 — Chapters 761 to 770, "The Thumb of the Shoulder", days 2683 to 2754

**This is the SECOND OF FIVE BLOCKS OF VOLUME 16 AND IT IS THE BLOCK WHERE THE FIRST OF MUDMONTH ARRIVES AND TAKES THE SHOULDER FOR NINE DAYS, and the prompt that produced it is `workspace/volume-16/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` and the successor of that prompt is Batch 0003 and not a volume close.** The block runs seventy-two days and carries two month boundaries, being the first of Mudmonth at day 2706 and the first of Longlight at day 2746. **The ten datelines are 2683, 2691, 2695, 2700, 2706, 2712, 2732, 2742, 2744 and 2754, being Thawmonth the eighth, the sixteenth, the twentieth and the twenty-fifth and Mudmonth the first, the seventh, the twenty-seventh, the thirty-seventh, the thirty-ninth and the forty-ninth of YR 323, and five of the ten are market mornings and five are not.** Every day of month and every weekday was derived from the day number and the single anchor at day 2310 and never copied out of the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` section seventeen, which is a certificate and not an authority.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID, IN THE ORDER IT DID IT.** It opened on the eighth of Thawmonth with a man from a mill lane four miles north saying out loud in a market street that his council had voted a driftway shut until the frost was off and that bars were being lifted off it on a Friday to get a cart through with paper in it, and the man of thirty-two asked him one question, which was how many households walk that driftway and will they say out loud what they want done with it. **THE ESCALATION THE SERIES ROW ASKS FOR IS MET HERE FROM THE GROUND AND NOT BY A FIGHT: he asked the nine households at their own doors before light the next morning, four miles through wet grass, and got nine answers that were the same, and at `chapter-0764.md` the nine said it again in a market street one by one in front of a hired carter, and the carter turned his empty cart and went back the way he had come.** No route was opened and none was shut and no cut was made anywhere. Then the first of Mudmonth came round on a Friday and about nine people in about nine trades stood before those boards one by one and said one thing out loud each and none of them touched the paper, and **NO GATE WAS SHUT IN THAT COUNTY THAT DAY AND NOBODY HAD ASKED THOSE NINE FOR ANYTHING AND NOBODY IS GOING TO.** Then nine days of water took the shoulder of that made road from the nineteenth of Mudmonth to the twenty-seventh, four of the nine people on that floor could not get there because the floor is four days off, and the number fell from eight to four and was said twice by five people in mist, and the block's finding is the sentence the man of thirty-two says out loud at a third door, which is that a practice which needs a made road is a practice that a person can switch off by ruining the road. **THE BLOCK ENDS ON A NEGATIVE BEAT AND NOT ON THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX: the first of Mudmonth came and gone with no roll made, no gate shut, and about four hundred people in a market street who do not know the roll exists.**

**THE RELATIONSHIP.** Two lanes and short words and nothing in a document. At `chapter-0763.md` she told him the two open mornings are for the room and the five shut ones are for the floor, and she knows he has never asked. **At `chapter-0769.md` he said that the lamp is a room and that he walks past it, and she said back that the room is his to pass and to come in dry, and neither of them called it anything.** The register gained no entry on any of the ten datelines, the day-book stands at eleven entries, the fourth ruled head is empty, no fifth head is ruled, and neither of them says a sentence about the four and a half years and nobody else comments on it. `Mara Vey` is printed once, at `chapter-0769.md`, and it is a return and costs nothing.

**WHAT IT COST, AND NONE OF IT IS CURED IN IT.** The use of one ear on the side the stick is on, which fills with rain, with stall noise, with damp and with a high thin ringing across the ten files, and the use of one leg, which goes from a dull ache to a sharp pull behind the knee to a dead weight and a knee that clicks on the next three steps, and the standing fact that he cannot be near the works without somebody standing with him. **AND ONE NEW THING, WHICH IS THAT THE PRACTICE HE BEGAN HAS A GROWTH RATE AND THAT THE GROWTH RATE IS NOT HIS TO SET, and it is said out loud at `chapter-0767.md` and again at `chapter-0769.md` about two mendings nobody asked for.** The two mendings are the roadkeeper's bar and the broom straw, both done on their own account in their own trades, and nobody in this county has ever asked a person to account for a thing they did on their own account and this was the first morning anybody could have and nobody did.

**THE ONE MARK THAT IS NOT A ROOM AND NOT A PAPER, WHICH IS THE FIRST OF THE THREE THINGS `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION ELEVEN HANDS ON.** At `chapter-0770.md` the man of about fifty-four went back four days up the made road with chalk on his thumb and made four short lines on the stone sill of the weigh-house door, where the rain does not reach and where no room is and no paper is, and they mean four, and nobody asked him to make them.

**THE MONEY, AND THE FINDING NOBODY HAD RUN, AND IT IS THE LARGEST FINDING OF THE BLOCK.** The ten totals are **61, 84, 190, 196, 201, 209, 214, 228, 240 and 255 pence, ten distinct and ascending, and every column foots four ways with the forty-eight-pence conversion checked separately from the sum, and the over-figures are 13, 36, 46, 4, 9, 17, 22, 36, none and 15, and no barred numeral is a price or an over-figure or a printed total in any of the ten.** **THE COLLISION SET WAS REBUILT WITH THE COMPLETE INSTRUMENT OVER ALL SEVEN HUNDRED AND SIXTY PRIOR FILES AND IT GIVES FOURTEEN FREE VALUES IN THE BAND AND TWO OF THEM CAN CARRY A COLUMN, being 61 and 84, and this block spent both of them, and the other eight totals are printed already and the closing paragraph of all ten files now says so on the page and gives the reason.** The figure of two hundred and fourteen usable values at `state/continuity.md` 1706 is false and was false when it was written, and Batch 0001's ten totals were all ten already printed in Volumes 11 to 14. **Over seven hundred and seventy files the usable free set in the band is now zero.** The language of the instrument is on no page.

**THE REVIEW OF THIS BLOCK AND WHAT IT COST TO ANSWER IT.** A reviewer read the ten files against `chapter-0758.md`, `chapter-0760.md` and the state file and reported twenty-eight findings. **TWELVE WERE REAL AND ALL TWELVE ARE FIXED**, being the wall gap printed as five feet on ten pages where the standing figure is nine, the practice-morning count and its derivation, a drove sentence that claimed two Thursdays inside nine days of water when one Thursday fell in them and a drove went up it, a first-of-a-month count that had incremented overnight, four duration conversions that were not durations, one lane beat dated to the wrong day, a coping lamp staged at the fifth hour and at the afternoon in the same paragraph, a clock that ran seven then five, a second clock and weather and cost that contradicted themselves three ways with an ungrammatical sentence in the middle of it, a narrator who called a lamp a household, a paragraph that opened on a standing negation, and a line of mine that put four people past a door on the same page as four hundred people hearing the same sayings. **Two more were done for cause and not for the review: six of the nine sayings at the first of Mudmonth are now given on the page in their own words instead of two, because a scene told by its count is not a scene, and the narration of the chosen household now says that a lamp at the foot of a stair is not a room until somebody says it is one.** Sixteen more findings were the house form, and the reason each was left alone is at `state/continuity.md` 1724. **THE BLOCK IS 306 WORDS LONGER AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR THAN BEFORE IT, and the growth is four more printed speeches and about three hundred words of named people in a scene that had been counted instead of written, and not one word of it is scaffold.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0003 — Chapters 771 to 780, "The Silence Is Not Death", days 2755 to 2826

**This is the THIRD OF FIVE BLOCKS OF VOLUME 16 AND IT IS THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL, AND THE BLOCK IS TWENTY CHAPTERS AND TWO BLOCKS AWAY FROM A VOLUME CLOSE, and the successor of this block is Batch 0004 and not a volume close.** The block runs seventy-two days and carries two month boundaries, being Longlight the first at day 2766 and Harvestmonth the first at day 2826, **and the second of them is the block's own last day.** **THE TEN DATELINES ARE 2755, 2760, 2766, 2772, 2779, 2783, 2791, 2800, 2811 AND 2826, being Mudmonth the fiftieth and fifty-fifth, Longlight the first, the seventh, the fourteenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-sixth, Rainmonth the fifth and the sixteenth, and Harvestmonth the first, all YR 323, and five of the ten are market mornings and five are not.** Every day of month and every weekday was derived from the day number and the single anchor at day 2310 and never copied out of the printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` section seventeen or the one at `outline/volume-16.md` section seventeen, which is wrong in four rows.

**WHAT THE BLOCK DID, IN THE ORDER IT DID IT.** It opened on the fiftieth of Mudmonth, four days up a made road at a weigh-house door, on four lines of chalk under the hood of that door where the rain does not reach and still sharp after nine days of rain that had been at the yard, and it put one question to the man of about fifty-four that nobody in nine counties has ever put to him, which is what the floor says when nobody is at it, and he answered in thirty-eight words that it says it and that a figure nobody has written down is a figure that dies with the man who said it and that stone is not a man. **THAT IS THE KNOWLEDGE RULE OF `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION FOURTEEN SATISFIED, being that somebody who matters already knew it and said something more careful, and it is said before the man of thirty-two has learned anything at all.** Then he walked eleven miles of made road north in four hours on a hot knee, before light, which is under the six a sound man takes, was stopped by a roadkeeper a mile short of the trough at the bottom of that cart track, which is not on the four miles the man keeps under a written paper, and told that he was not a guest, and asked him, in that man's trade, the first question anybody in this county has ever put to a person about a thing they did on their own account, and got nineteen words back. Then on the first of Longlight, the seventeenth first of a month in a row, he went up a cart track to the head of it in daylight and counted nine hurdles foot to foot with a hurdle shoe on the top one and a thumb of lime gone to powder, and four hundred yards back nine houses with no smoke off any of them, and knocked twice on the nearest door and got about nine seconds of nothing. He came down that hill lighter than he went up, **AND HE WAS WRONG, AND THE WRONGNESS WAS THAT HE HAD DECIDED ON THE TUESDAY WHAT HE WAS GOING TO FIND ON THE MONDAY.** On the seventh of Longlight he went up again, stood nine feet short of those nine hurdles for about three hours, and at about the ninth hour one of the four hundred yards of ground put a light in one window of the fourth house, and he walked down and knocked twice and a voice came to the inside of the door and did not open and said twenty-nine words and gave him a name. **THE REVERSAL IS PAID HERE AND IT IS NOT A COMFORT: the hurdles went in by those households' own hands and they would like them to stay, because a shut road with no name against it is the only thing the Marshal's hand in that notice cannot use, and that is the whole of what nine people have.** On the fourteenth of Longlight he came down the eleven miles and a man at a stone trough at the bottom of that track, who fills it twice a week and carries water up on his own back, said out loud that he counts what does not come up, and asked him seven words and gave him twenty-six words: write it on something and put it where the rain cannot get at it and do not tell me where. **Then he did not say it in a market street, because the street had about nine people in it, and he learned which streets are rooms and which are about nine people in the open air.** On the eighteenth of Longlight he stood four hours on that floor and was not one of the two, and the volume's one System panel is spent on that hour, and he stood in the gap in the wall and put his hand on the near end of the eight finished yards and took it off. On the twenty-sixth, a market morning, he said the whole of it out loud at a table to about four hundred people in forty-two words, and about nine of them stopped, and then he said twenty-nine more words to say that he was not going to tell anybody to lift a hurdle. On the fifth of Rainmonth a keeper of records wrote a figure down in her own hand on the back leaf of a day-book that is not the record, behind a shut door, for about nine minutes, and told him he would not read it and would not tell her he had. On the sixteenth of Rainmonth the woman who keeps the inn asked him for a figure in her own trade and he had not got one, and her board went back under her arm with nine blanks on it. And on the first of Harvestmonth, the eighteenth first of a month in a row, a roadkeeper said at the boards that a bar will get up that track if a barrow can and that he is not going to be the one who tries it, and a man of thirty-two stood nine feet off a shut door with a book behind it and neither of them said anything at all.

**THE MONEY, AND THE SET IS STILL EXHAUSTED.** The ten totals are **85, 113, 142, 163, 186, 208, 229, 250, 274 and 297 pence, ten distinct and ascending, all ten printed already, and every column foots four ways with the forty-eight-pence conversion checked separately from the sum, and the over-figures are 37, 17, 46, 19, 42, 16, 37, 10, 34 and 9, and no barred numeral is a price or an over-figure or a printed total in any of the ten.** **THE COLLISION SET WAS REBUILT WITH THE COMPLETE INSTRUMENT OVER ALL SEVEN HUNDRED AND SEVENTY FILES THAT STOOD BEFORE THIS BLOCK AND IT GIVES TWELVE FREE VALUES IN THE BAND AND NOT ONE OF THEM CAN CARRY A COLUMN, being 212, 218, 230, 254, 266, 273, 281, 308, 314, 321, 326 and 329 with over-figures 20, 26, 38, 14, 26, 33, 41, 20, 26, 33, 38 and 41, every one of them one of the barred six.** **Each of the ten closing paragraphs says on the page that the band from sixty to three hundred and forty-three has been searched whole again and gives twelve values that no file in the manuscript before them has printed, and that every one of the twelve would give an over-figure that cannot be priced, and that the ten totals of the ten columns are printed already, and that the reason is arithmetic, and that the route below sixty is shut.** The language of the instrument is on no page and the four permitted phrases stand exactly once in each of the ten closing paragraphs.

**WHAT IT COST, AND NONE OF IT IS CURED IN IT.** The use of one ear on the side the stick is on, which takes rain, drip, market noise, six hundred people and seven people breathing in the dark, and which the block states as a half deafness he will have on that side when this is over. The use of one leg, which takes eleven miles of made road in four hours before light and about nine otherwise, two trips up a cart track, about three hours standing on a rise, a knee that goes down once on the seventh of Longlight and gets him up on the second attempt with nobody seeing it but him, and four hours on a stone floor. The standing fact that he cannot be near the works without somebody standing with him. **And two new costs, both of them named on the page and neither of them a vision: that a silence he can now name is not a thing he can carry, and that being the man who went and looked is a job and not an honour. And the cost of the finding is that he cannot turn it into a number: a woman who keeps an inn asked him for one in her own trade and a board with nine blanks on it stayed blank.**

**THE NAMES.** Two of the volume's six are spent, `Wyn Fell` at `chapter-0774.md` and `Abel Chinn` at `chapter-0775.md`, one in each. `Mara Vey` is printed once at `chapter-0778.md` and is a return. **The antagonist's name is printed zero times across the thirty files of Volume 16 so far.** The protagonist is thirty-two and was not recounted.

**THE MEASUREMENTS, RECOUNTED ON THE FILES AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR PASS AT `state/continuity.md` 1741 AND NOT INHERITED, WITH THE COLUMN BEFORE THE PASS BESIDE IT.** The per-file word counts are **4,031 / 4,251 / 4,045 / 4,041 / 4,055 / 4,026 / 3,665 / 4,241 / 3,998 / 4,062**, the block total is **40,415**, the mean is **4,041.5 and 4,041 truncated**, the minimum is 3,665 at `chapter-0777.md` and the maximum is 4,251 at `chapter-0772.md`, and no file is below three thousand words, **and they were 3,940 / 4,054 / 4,039 / 4,035 / 4,010 / 4,017 / 3,654 / 4,229 / 3,992 / 4,045 for a total of 40,015 and a mean of 4,001.5 before the pass.** `about` is **23.57 / 24.94 / 26.45 / 22.52 / 21.45 / 23.10 / 25.10 / 22.40 / 25.76 / 22.90** per 1,000, floor at `chapter-0775.md` and ceiling at `chapter-0773.md`, and no page is at or above 27.00.

**THE CENSUS, ON THE NAMED INSTRUMENT AT `state/continuity.md` 1708, AND UNCHANGED BY THE REPAIR PASS BECAUSE THE PASS ADDED AND REMOVED NO PARAGRAPH.** **Basis A 353 paragraphs and ZERO openers and Basis B 299 and ZERO, against Volume 15's five blocks at 83.4, 91.1, 89.6, 80.6 and 56.4 on Basis B, and the Basis B denominator is now re-derivable from FIVE named exclusions and not two, being 353 less ten datelines less twenty-three paragraphs that open with a quotation mark less the one System panel, which is its own paragraph and is neither a dateline nor dialogue, less the ten bold cost codas, less the ten bold closing money columns.** The thank family is **thirty-seven** uses and every one of the thirty-seven is a negation of thanking or a plain statement that a person was not thanked, and nobody is thanked anywhere in the block, **and the thirty-six this entry printed before the repair pass was one short on the files.** The build vocabulary is zero and interiority is present in all ten files. **The duplicate count over all seven hundred and eighty files is thirty-six groups and thirty-seven surplus occurrences and THIS BLOCK ADDS ZERO OF BOTH, having begun the pass at seven groups and eighteen surplus occurrences and been cut back by varying five stock paragraphs on nine datelines, and the same instrument over the seven hundred and seventy prior files gives the same thirty-six and thirty-seven, which is the check that the zero is a zero.** All twenty-three printed speeches foot off the words printed beside them and the four speeches that carry no count carry no count.
